Recon Build Spec

Version 0 · epic #466 · 2026-10-26 · rendered from specs/recon/ at 3334bc8. An agent reads the rules, the root, its one task file and the code merged on main, and nothing else.

The models, the agents, thinking effort and the operator, measured. One crawler crawls every line the kernel captured, every repository those lines lead to and every host those repositories live on, in depth, into one structure, and the module reports on it in its own tab, on its own port. The crawler is the only thing in the module that reads: it finds the details that are not readily apparent, ties what the agents did to what became of their work, and delivers the critical data no one knew to look for.

The spec pack

Every box is a file of the repository; open one to read it as the repository holds it. The arrows are how the files name each other. A build starts at its task file, reads the root, then the rules, then its task file whole.

The sequence
Four tasks, four PRs at most: the structure first, whole, before anything fills it; then the crawler that fills it, the lines in one task and the repositories with their hosts in the next; then the tab, designed with the operator once the crawler is merged. Each task is one build an agent does from its file; the operator deploys and runs what it builds. A task starts when the task before it is merged on main. Its file under tasks/ carries, verbatim, what the structure and the authority files say about its piece, what it builds, its gates and what it owns; this list carries the piece, the files it reflects and what it builds.
#TaskNameThe pieceBuildsAfter
1#536structureThe module becomes superx-mod-recon, and its structure is built whole before anything fills it: the crate, from superx-mod-performance, its code moved and renamed and its crawler doing what it does today; the executable superx-recon; the database superx/recon, from the structure, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, which the crate applies byte for byte and never writes: the deep crawl's tables as approved, the hosts' and the mirrors' tables, every answer of every read API kept whole beside typed tables for what is measured, and git's facts; the parameters attr_recon_*; the module folder with its repos folder and its credentials file; the status, listing every table and which hosts have credentials. superx-mod-performance and superx-mod-repos leave the workspace, and the core UI links to no module's own UI (#534).crates/superx-mod-recon/
2#537linesThe crawler crawls the captured lines in depth. Every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, is read once into the tables it feeds, and is itself a line row that names those tables, none when it fed none; every field seen on a kind of line is a line_field row that says whether a column holds it; every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same range, and a difference makes the pass partial and is named. It fills the lines' tables whole: sessions and their places, prompts, triggers, replies and what they said, their states, thinking, tool calls and their results, edits, git events, tests and builds, turns, compactions, recaps, requests with their pieces and shapes, context, hooks, session states, costs by session and by model, links, git states, notices, questions, task changes, plans, subagent runs and queue changes.crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/t01
3#538repositoriesThe crawler crawls the repositories and their hosts. From the working directories the lines name, and the roots the operator names, every checkout and repository; one worker per repository, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows. For each repository its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, and every fact git holds: commits with their files, renames and copies, their time zones, patch ids, signatures and trailers; how each merge's conflicts were resolved; annotated tags; refs with their upstreams and how far each is ahead and behind, and every reflog entry; HEAD moves and operations in progress; where each commit sits and what landed through another commit, what each commit still holds at the main line's tip, the files at the tip, uncommitted work, remotes and set-up, and every git call. From the hosts, with the credentials and GET only, what the hosts' tables hold. Every commit tied to the session that made it.crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/t02
4#539tabThe tab: what the module shows on its own port, from what t02 and t03 filled. Designed with the operator once t03 is merged (decision the-tab).crates/superx-mod-recon/t03
The decisions
What was decided, when, and where it lives; what is still open. A confirmed decision is written into the file or element named, which is the authority; it is listed here so an agent knows a question is closed. An open decision is a question an agent never answers: a task that needs it stops. An open decision names in closes-before the task before whose file it is closed, and check.py refuses that file while the decision is open.
DecisionStatusWhenWhatWhere it lives

one-module-one-crawlerconfirmed2026-10-25One module, one schema, one crawler: the lines and the repositories are crawled by one crawler into one database, through its sources, and a new source plugs in beside them.BLUEPRINT.md; rules one-module-its-own-process
own-processconfirmed2026-10-24Its own executable, process, database, port and tab, never linked into superx; it reads the kernel's data and the kernel never depends on it.rules one-module-its-own-process
crawler-is-the-readerconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler is the only thing that reads; nothing beside it, and no part of it, is called a reader.rules crawler-is-the-reader
crawl-not-skimconfirmed2026-10-25Slow, diligent, methodical: every line, field, commit and ref accounted for and checked against its source; the details that are not readily apparent found and tied together.rules crawl-not-skim
repos-from-the-logsconfirmed2026-10-25The repositories come from the session logs; the logs report a change first and the crawler follows them.rules repos-from-the-logs
hosts-and-mirrorsconfirmed2026-10-26The credentials serve both: the hosts' APIs, read-only, and the crawler's own mirror clones, fetched with them. The approved structure's sentence that git is read with never a fetch holds for the operator's checkouts; t01 rewrites it to say the mirrors are fetched and nothing else is.rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials, never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t01, t03
repos-folderconfirmed2026-10-26The mirror clones live in a folder of their own, under the module's folder, which nothing else writes.identity data
mirrors-take-everythingconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler fetches into its own mirrors freely: one mirror per remote, a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, and nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, so a branch rewritten or deleted on the host keeps what the crawler saw. The more it holds, the more it can find.rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03
parallel-by-repositoryconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler scales by repository: one worker per repository, as many at once as a parameter allows.rules parallel-by-repository; sequence t03
structure-firstconfirmed2026-10-26The structure first, whole; then the crawler that fills it; then the tab, designed after.sequence
four-prsconfirmed2026-10-26Four PRs at most; fewer PRs, more work in each.sequence
specs-are-the-truthconfirmed2026-10-26The XML files are the source of truth, in the data lake's pack: a root, the rules, one file per task, and the tools that check and render them; a branch and a PR are built from a task file.rules specs-are-the-truth; identity specs
deep-structureconfirmed2026-10-25The deep crawl's tables, approved whole: every field the agents write and everything git holds gets a column, a table, or a stated reason to stay in the kernel line.crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
old-dataconfirmed2026-10-25The kernel's lines captured before a capture fix are read as they are: nothing is recaptured or repaired.rules derived-and-rebuilt
hosts-take-everythingconfirmed2026-10-26From the hosts, everything their read APIs return for each repository the crawler crawls: every answer kept whole, beside typed tables for what is measured, pull requests, their commits, reviews, comments, checks and pipelines, merges, issues, releases and the rest the answers hold, and every call recorded. Less data is more assumption; the operator has intuition already, and the crawler exists for the facts the operator does not know. The tables themselves are designed into the structure from the hosts' own API documentation, and the operator approves the structure (§7, §11).sequence t01, t03; rules crawl-not-skim
credentials-fileconfirmed2026-10-26<home>/modules/recon/credentials.json, in JSON as the instance's own files are, written by the operator: one entry per host, each a token with every read scope the host offers and no write scope; the crawler refuses the file when anyone but its owner can read it. A mirror's fetch gets its credential from superx-recon itself, answering git as its askpass program, so a token is never written into git's configuration, a URL, a command line or the environment.authority credentials; rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials
live-gatesconfirmed2026-10-26A task's live gates run before its merge, on the operator's instance, once the operator has deployed the branch's build of superx-recon; superx and its daemon are never touched, and every row the build writes is derived, so the next deploy rebuilds what it needs.method gates, deploy
retire-performanceconfirmed2026-10-26At t01's deploy superx-performance is stopped and superx-recon started in its place; once recon's first pass is complete, the operator drops superx/performance and superx/repos under root. Every row they hold is derived, and recon derives it again from the same lines and repositories.sequence t01
repos-at-onceconfirmed2026-10-26attr_recon_repos_at_once falls back to no limit: every repository a pass crawls gets its own worker at once, unless the operator sets a number. A limit's fallback is no limit.rules parallel-by-repository, nothing-dropped
structureconfirmed2026-10-26The structure as designed on 2026-10-26: the deep crawl's tables as approved, the changes to tables 26, 28, 30, 31, 33, 55 and 59, and the hosts' and mirrors' tables 60–82, read from the hosts' own API documentation and checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4, a good row of every table taken and every bad row refused by the rule it breaks. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26 (§7, §11). It lives where CI guards a module's schema, so every change to it carries the operator's approval marker.crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
git-factsconfirmed2026-10-26Git's own documentation read whole (git 2.50.1), and what git holds that the structure lacked added to it: where each commit was made in local time, files copied beside files moved, how each merge's conflicts were resolved, the commits that make the same change, operations a checkout is in the middle of, each branch's upstream and how far it is ahead and behind, line counts blind to whitespace, annotated tags and signatures, commit trailers; tables 83–85 and changes to tables 18, 21, 22, 28, 30, 31 and 56, checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26: "1 -9 yes change the schema for more data".crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql
git-cliconfirmed2026-10-26The crawler reads git through git itself, one process per call, never a library: every call is a git_call row, and git's answers are git's own. The operator, 2026-10-26: "Recommendation: keep the CLI. yes".rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03
http-clientconfirmed2026-10-26The hosts' APIs are called through reqwest, with rustls, the one HTTP client for both hosts: no host's own client library, since every answer is kept whole with its headers, and no maintained Bitbucket Cloud client exists. Its redirects drop the credential when they leave the host. Recommended with the git CLI and answered with the same yes.sequence t03

It depends on superx-kernel alone, and no crate of the workspace depends on it. The operator: the kernel is off limits to a module's workThe crawler reads the kernel's database with SELECT and nothing else, a bounded page at a time, in the order of its rows' ids, as the service account the instance names; it never writes it and never reads it through another client. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; what the module needs from the kernel it reads as the kernel writes it.review of every query the module issues; tools/skill_audit.py (§10)

the-structure-decidescrates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "Nothing here is judged." The operator, 2026-10-26: first build the structure, then the crawler that fills itThe structure, the module's one schema file, says every table, field, link, index and vocabulary the crawler writes, and a test reads the schema back out of the engine and diffs it against the file. Nothing the crawler writes is outside it. A task changes it only as its file says; any other change is designed with the operator and approved per change (§7, §11), and a task that needs one stops.the schema test of the module; CI's Operator-approved marker on a schema file; review
crawler-is-the-readerThe operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler is the reader; there are no readers beside itOne crawler reads everything the module holds: the lines, the repositories, the hosts. Nothing else in the module reads a source. A source is a part of the crawler, named for what it crawls, never a component beside it; nothing in the module, its code, its tests, its specs or its PRs is called a reader.specs/recon/check.py (the word in a spec); review
crawl-not-skimThe operator, 2026-10-25: "crawler means slow diligent methodical"; "we crawl not skim". 2026-10-26: the crawler finds the details that are not readily apparent, and is counted on to deliver critical data no one was aware ofThe crawler crawls. Every field a source carries gets a column or a table, or a line_field row that says no column holds it. Every line read is a line row that names the tables it fed, none when it fed none. Every repository is read whole: all of its history, every ref, every reflog, every blame. Every pass checks what it read against what its source holds, the kernel's lines in the range read, git's own counts, a host's totals, and records any difference; a pass that finds one is partial, the difference in its note. Speed is no goal; pacing is, and capture comes first.the tests of t02 and t03; their live gates (passes complete, the lines read equal to the lines held); review
nothing-droppedcrates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs: "The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471)." The operator, 2026-10-24: design to the requirement, never to the diskNo sampling, no cap, no truncation, no summary: a limit's fallback is no limit; a value is kept as the source wrote it, in the column the structure gives it; a line, a field or a value the crawler cannot place is counted and named, never dropped and never guessed at.the tests of every task; review
derived-and-rebuiltcrates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql: "EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,". The operator, 2026-10-25: the lines captured before today are read as they are, never fixedEvery row is derived from the kernel's lines, git and the hosts, and a provision rebuilds the whole database from them; the one exception is what git or a host has since expired or pruned and the crawler had kept, which the structure names. A line captured before a capture fix is read as it is: the crawler never recaptures, repairs or fills in a kernel line.review; the live gates after a provision
repos-from-the-logsThe operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler builds its list from the logs, which are the first to report a change. 2026-10-26: the crawler discovers the repositories from the session logs, then goes from the sessions into themThe repositories come from the logs: every working directory a session's lines name is followed to the checkout and the repository that hold it, and so is every root the operator names in attr_recon_include; a commit or a push a session printed sends the crawler to its repository in the same pass. A repository no session and no root leads to is never crawled, and one the operator excludes in attr_recon_exclude never is. A checkout whose folder is gone is recorded gone and keeps its history.the tests of t03; review
never-write-a-measured-repositoryThe operator, 2026-10-13 (#446): the product never writes to a repository it measures, or runs code in one. 2026-10-26: a dedicated folder for the repositories, under the module, into which the crawler fetches freely: the more it holds, the betterThe operator's repositories are read with git, read-only: never a write, a fetch, a checkout, a lock, or a program a repository names (no diff driver, no external diff, no signature check, no fsmonitor, optional locks off). Fetching happens only into the crawler's own mirror clones, in its repos folder, which nothing else writes: a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, and a mirror's hooks never run.the tests of the module's git calls, which read every call's arguments and environment; review
hosts-read-only-with-credentialsThe operator, 2026-10-25: the crawler gets a credentials file for the hosts. 2026-10-26: the credentials serve the hosts and the mirror clones bothThe hosts are read with GET only, with the operator's read-only credentials from the credentials file. The file is mode 600 and read by the crawler alone, and the crawler refuses a file others can read. A credential is never stored, logged, printed, shown on the tab, written into a URL, a command line, the environment, git's configuration or a mirror, and never sent to a host other than the one it is for; git asks superx-recon for it when it fetches.the tests of t01 and t03; specs/recon/check.py (no credential written in a spec); review
parallel-by-repositoryThe operator, 2026-10-26: the crawler scales by crawling more repositories, more of them at once, the number set by a parameterThe crawler crawls one repository per worker, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows, each worker making its own git and host calls; within a repository its steps run in their order, and a repository's facts are written by its own worker alone. The lines are crawled as one stream, in the order the kernel captured them. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself.the tests of t03; review
capture-firstBLUEPRINT.md: "It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture." The operator, 2026-10-25: capturing correct, whole data is the kernel's purposeNothing the crawler does slows or stops capture: it reads the kernel's database a bounded page at a time, holds nothing open between pages, and reads nothing while attr_recon_read_secs is 0; a pass that cannot read waits for the next.the live gates, read beside capture's own ticks; review
no-hardcoded-policy.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Facts go in columns; judgments do not."Every tunable is a parameter attr_recon_*, with its fallback in the module's params.rs, reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape; a constant that is no policy carries an audited skill-allow marker. Judgments, such as which work the operator directed, what is rework and how work is split into sittings, are parameters applied when a chart is drawn, never columns and never constants.tools/skill_audit.py (§9); review
types-flow.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "A `record<table>` is a `Thing` everywhere."Types flow end to end: a link is a record id, a kernel line's id a Uuid, a time a DateTime<Utc>, from the line to the column; nothing is cast to text to pass the engine, and a refusal of the engine is fixed in the code, never by loosening the structure (§12, §14).tools/skill_audit.py (§14); the structure's own asserts; review
gates-are-the-truth.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes."A task is done when cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings and python3 tools/skill_audit.py pass and every gate of its file is green; nothing is called done, ready or complete before, and a gate that cannot fail is no gate.CI's required checks; the PR's gate table
trunk.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Every change ties to a GitHub issue"Each task is one issue, one branch cut from the head of main and one PR into main, merged by the operator; one open branch and one open PR at a time; a merged branch is deleted.review
specs-are-the-truthThe operator, 2026-10-26: "xmls are the source of truth"The XML files under specs/recon/ are the source: the issues and the Build Spec page are rendered from them (build_issues.py, build_page.py) and nothing flows back but an issue's number. A spec file is committed only when specs/recon/check.py passes on it, and the check's mutation test proves the check catches what it guards.specs/recon/check.py; specs/recon/mutate_check.py
one-agentThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's ruling of 2026-10-03 for every build from a specOne session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds a task does every step itself: reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed. This rule is a constraint of every task.review of the PR's history
one-build-per-taskThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's rulings of 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-05 for every build from a specEach version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch, one PR; the operator reviews and merges before the next task starts. The next task's agent reads what the root's method read lists and nothing of an unmerged branch. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes is it built by several agents, one per model, each from the same file version on its own branch; none is merged until the operator chooses one.review
agent-decides-only-what-is-listedThe operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is; the operator, 2026-10-08: "what is left to the agent's determination? not much: we are building the defined product"Every task file lists under agent-decides the only things its agent decides: the names and the decomposition inside its own modules and tests, the means of a step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies, the contents of its fixtures within the cases listed, its commit granularity. Everything else a build needs is written in the task file, the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code; a build that decides anything else, or decides it differently from the file, is closed, and the step it departed from is named in the review. Every gate names the steps it proves, so a failed gate points at the step to look at.review of the PR against the task file's agent-decides list and its steps; the gates, each naming the steps it proves
kickoffThe operator, 2026-10-26: a branch and a PR are built from the task's XML file; the operator may name the session that builds a task, the spec's own includedA build starts with a model pointed at one task file in the repository, specs/recon/tasks/<issue>.xml. Nothing else is said at kickoff. The kickoff line is the first message of a session opened for that build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin: not the session that wrote the spec, not a session that built or reviewed another build, not one that holds a conversation. There, before the branch is cut, python3 tools/skill_audit.py run on the head of main names what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head, no more and no fewer: it reads no file on a path through a folder named worktrees, and every file elsewhere in the folder, whether git holds it or not; a folder where it names fewer or more is not a build folder, and the build stops there. A model whose session holds anything before the kickoff line beyond what the harness gives every session (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set the model or the effort, with its output) stops at that line, says so, and builds nothing. The model writes its own label, <model>-<effort>, as the root's method model gives it. The operator may instead name a session to build a task, the spec's own included: that session builds when the operator says so, from the task file as written, reads the files in the order the root's method read gives from that word on, and its PR says it was the session the operator named. The file's start element names the branch, the PR title and the first word of every commit; a build started any other way, or on a branch or PR named any other way, does not count.review of the branch name and the PR title against the task file's start element; the PR body stating that the kickoff line was the session's first message, and on which checkout
every-rule-bindsThese rulesEvery rule here binds every task. Every gate of a task names in its proves attribute what it proves; a rule that no gate of a task names binds the task all the same.specs/recon/check.py
specs/recon/rules.xml, the XML as written
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!--
  SuperX Recon: the build rules. Version 0.

  One file, no generator. Every rule has three parts:
    from    - where the rule comes from: a sentence of an authority file (quoted, checked verbatim by
              specs/recon/check.py), the merged code, or the operator's ruling with its date
    text    - the rule, as it binds every task and every agent
    breach  - what catches a breach, each a guard that exists or that a task of the sequence builds:
              specs/recon/check.py over the specs, the lints (clippy, tools/skill_audit.py) over the code,
              a test, a gate of a task, or the operator's review
  Nothing here writes a count of the captured lines, the sessions, the repositories or the hosts.
-->
<rules product="SuperX Recon" epic="466" version="0" date="2026-10-26">

It depends on superx-kernel alone, and no crate of the workspace depends on it. The kernel never waits on it: a recon that is stopped, slow or broken leaves capture untouched.</text>
