    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/" name="the recon module">
      <step n="1" name="the move">Every file of crates/superx-mod-performance moves to crates/superx-mod-recon with git mv, keeping its history, except schema/performance.surql, which is removed with git rm: the structure that replaces it is crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, already there. Cargo.toml: the package superx-mod-recon, its description naming the recon module and what it measures, its one binary superx-recon from src/main.rs, its dependencies unchanged.</step>
      <step n="2" name="the structure">lib.rs: SCHEMA_DDL is include_str!("../schema/recon.surql") and MODULE_NAME is "recon", so the module's database is superx/recon and its account superx_mod_recon (crates/superx-kernel/src/substrate.rs module_db), its registry entry recon, its folder &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/. The structure only adds to the tables the crawler writes and to the words they take, so the crawler's code writes what it writes today, unchanged.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the names">Every name the module shows takes recon: the executable superx-recon in its command line, its help, its messages and its errors; its pid file superx-recon.pid and its output log superx-recon.out in the module folder; daemon.rs's executable name superx-recon, a running process told by its name as ps prints it, so names_this_executable accepts superx-recon alone, the Linux cut of a long name keeping the rule it has today for any name it would cut; the Host guard's refusal, status's first line and own_hint's provisioning command name superx-recon; every tracing target "performance" becomes "recon". The doc comments say recon. None says the core UI links to the module: the module publishes its URL in the registry (attr_module_ui_url, unchanged), and the core UI does not read it after step 11.</step>
      <step n="4" name="the parameters">Every attr_performance_&lt;name&gt; becomes attr_recon_&lt;name&gt;: the same names after the prefix, in the same order, with the same fallbacks, the same shapes and the same reports. No parameter is added or removed. The struct params.rs reads the set values with takes a name other than `reader`.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the module folder">At run, after the module is registered as starting and before it says it is active: the module folder holds repos/, made with mode 700 when it is absent and left as it is, mode included, when it is present; and credentials.json, written when it is absent, with mode 600, holding the template, and never written when it is present, whatever it holds. The template is this JSON object, written by serde_json::to_string_pretty with a newline after it: "_doc" with the text "Read-only tokens, one per host. github.com: a fine-grained personal access token with read access to metadata, contents, pull requests, issues, actions, commit statuses, deployments and administration. bitbucket.org: an API token with the read scopes. Keep this file mode 600. superx-recon reads it, never writes it once it exists, and never shows a token."; "github.com" with the object holding "token": ""; "bitbucket.org" with the object holding "token": "". Instance gains the two paths, &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/repos and &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, beside the pid file and the logs.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the credentials">A module of the crate reads credentials.json, when status renders and when a part of the crawler asks for a host's token (t03). Its answer is one of: none, the file is absent; refused, the file's mode lets anyone but its owner read or write it (its mode and 0o077 is not 0), and nothing of it is read; unreadable, the file is not a JSON object whose every key that does not start with "_" names an object with a string "token", with serde_json's reason or the key that broke it; or the hosts, each github.com or bitbucket.org, with its token or no token when the string is empty, and every other key that does not start with "_" named as an unknown host. A key that starts with "_" is a note and is skipped. A token is held in a type whose Debug and Display print &lt;token&gt; and nothing of the token, and no log line, status text, error or test output ever holds one.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the status">status lists every table of the structure but pass and pass_source, each once, in groups, each table with the name its rows are called: sessions, context, models and tools as today; repos as today, and after its tables merge_resolution "merge resolutions", tag "tags" and commit_trailer "trailers"; then hosts: host_repository "repositories on hosts", host_repository_state "host settings", host_user "host accounts", host_crawl "host crawls", host_call "host calls", host_object "host objects", mirror "mirrors", pull_request "pull requests", pull_request_state "pull request states", pull_request_commit "pull request commits", pull_request_file "pull request files", pull_request_review "reviews", pull_request_event "pull request events", issue "issues", issue_state "issue states", host_comment "comments", ci_run "CI runs", ci_run_state "CI run states", ci_log "CI logs", release "releases", host_rule "host rules", deployment "deployments", deployment_state "deployment states"; then census as today. After the groups and before the last pass, two lines: `credentials &lt;path&gt;: ` and then, when the answer of step 6 is the hosts, github.com then bitbucket.org, each `&lt;host&gt; token set` or `&lt;host&gt; no token`, then each unknown host as `&lt;host&gt; unknown`, joined by ` · `; when it is none, `none`; refused, `refused, mode &lt;its mode in octal&gt;; chmod 600 &lt;path&gt;`; unreadable, `unreadable: &lt;the reason&gt;`. Then `repos       &lt;path&gt;`, the repos folder.</step>
      <step n="8" name="the words">No file of the crate calls anything a `reader`: the doc comments that do, in crawler/value.rs, crawler/lines/mod.rs, crawler/lines/facts.rs, crawler/lines/kernel.rs, crawler/lines/state.rs, crawler/lines/formats/mod.rs and crawler/lines/formats/claude_code.rs, say the crawler or what it does, their meaning unchanged.</step>
      <step n="9" name="the tests">The crate's tests move with it and take the new names. tests/schema.rs's contract becomes the structure's: every table the structure defines, with its fields and indexes, written out in the test as today, and the account superx_mod_recon. Every refusal it holds today stands but one: the step fetch, which the structure makes valid; that case takes the step push. The new rules are proven as the tests element lists them.</step>

piece
The lines task. The crawler crawls every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, side files and lines that are not JSON included, in depth. Each line is a line row naming the tables it fed. Each field a kind of line carries is a line_field row saying whether a column holds it. Every table the structure gives the lines is filled from the shapes the lines are on record in, and the tables the first crawler fills gain the columns the structure added to them. A reply read again steps over the lines between its own. Every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same ids. Nothing of the repositories, their mirrors or their hosts is crawled here: that is t03.
before
Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, on the operator's instance superx-recon running on superx/recon. Its crawler does what superx-performance's did. It reads the kernel's message rows and, of its telemetry, Claude Code's system lines turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary and its queued_command attachments. It writes session; session_place; prompt, all but prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index; reply, all but request, prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed; reply_state, all but cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens; thinking; tool_call, all but skill, model and timeout_ms; tool_result, all but decision, decided_by, decision_source, decision_reason, denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note, background_task and text; edit, from the file tools alone; git_event, test_run, build_run, turn, compaction and away. And it lists the repositories. Every other table of the lines is empty.
after
Who uses what this task builds: t03 (#538), which follows the sessions' places, git events, git states and links to the repositories and their hosts, and ties commits to sessions; t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table this task fills.

build
module · the lines part of the crawler
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/
step · every line
n
1
Each pass reads, in the order of their ids as today and in pages as bounded as today's, every message row, its raw_text beside its raw, and every telemetry_stream row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event, its payload whole, a side file's content included; every other telemetry row is no line and is skipped, as today. A line is read from its raw_text, parsed as JSON, wherever the kernel kept one, and from its parsed copy, a message row's raw or a payload's line, only where the kernel kept no raw_text: the kernel kept the parsed copy alone before #529, both from #529, the raw_text alone while #542 was its build, and both again from #546, and a line kept in any of these shapes goes through the same steps below. A side file and a Claude Desktop record are read from their payload's line, as today. A Gemini transcript_event of kind superseded holds an earlier write of a user or gemini record, whole, and is read as that record is read, in its place among the lines. Each agent's format knows the kinds of line the steps below read, and these, which feed no table: Claude Code's file-history-snapshot, file-history-delta, last-prompt, atis-latch, agent-name, fork-context-ref, started, launched, result, failed, artifact-autoreact-ledger and artifact-comment-monitor lines and its side files; Gemini's header and $set lines; every Claude Desktop record. A line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON, and a line in which a format finds a shape it does not read, as today, is counted unread.
step · the line
n
2
Every line read is one line row, written once ever: when the line is first read, as that read found it, and after every other row that read wrote from it. For each page it reads, the crawler asks its database which of the page's kernel ids line already holds; a line read again writes no line row, though it may write a fact its first read could not, such as one of a session the kernel has named since. kernel_message: its id. at: as today, the line's own time where it has one, else when the kernel captured it. agent: the agent's name the kernel gives it; a line the kernel names no agent for is no line row, and is counted unread. session: the session row of the session the kernel names for it, NONE where it names none, as for a Claude Desktop record or a project's own side file. kind: the line's type; for a Claude Code system line its type, a slash and its subtype, and for an attachment its type, a slash and its attachment's type; where the line holds no type, the kind the kernel gave it (a Gemini header or $set, a line kept by its kind alone before #471); a Claude Code line that is not JSON, transcript_raw; a Claude Desktop record, desktop_event. agent_uuid: its uuid. parent_uuid: its parentUuid, else its logicalParentUuid. prompt_id: its promptId. sidechain: its isSidechain, false where it has none. client_version: its version. bytes: the length in bytes of the line's raw_text, where the kernel kept one; for a side file, the bytes its line gives; for a Claude Desktop record, the length in bytes of its text; for a line kept by its kind alone, 0; for a line captured before the kernel kept raw_text, the length of the line as captured written out as compact JSON (value::json_len). read_as: the tables this read wrote a row to from the line, each once, in the order the structure's read_as list gives them; empty when it wrote none.
step · the fields
n
3
Every field of every line that is JSON is counted by its path. Each (agent, kind, path) is one line_field row the first time a line shows it, first_at the line's at, first_line its kernel id, stored whether a column holds it; and one row more each time stored changes for it. The crawler holds the newest row of each, read from its database at its first pass, and writes one only when it differs. A path is the keys from the line's top to the field, joined by a dot. A list's items take the list's path with [] after it. A block of a message's content list is named by its type in brackets, message.content[text]; a tool call by its name, message.content[tool_use:Bash]; a tool result by the name of the call it answers, message.content[tool_result:Bash], with ? where the crawler holds no such call; a line's toolUseResult the same way, toolUseResult[Bash]. A key is written <k> when it is data: a key that is not a name, a name being made of ASCII letters, digits, _, $ and - and beginning with a letter, _ or $; a key that is a UUID; and every key of a map the agent's format names as keyed by data, for Claude Code modelUsage, snapshot.trackedFileBackups, artifacts, wireToolInputs and wireIngestContext, the answers and the annotations of an AskUserQuestion result, and the files of an Artifact call's input. Each agent's format declares, per kind, the paths whose values its reading puts in a column, a path whose value a column holds whole standing for itself and every path under it; those paths are stored, and every other is not.
step · the operator's prompts
n
4
A prompt is read as today, and takes the columns the structure added: prompt_id its promptId, source its promptSource, permission_mode its permissionMode, turn_index and prompt_index its turnPosition.turnIndex and turnPosition.promptIndex, each NONE where the line has none.
step · the triggers
n
5
A line that is no prompt as today's prompts are read is a trigger row when the first of these holds. A user line that is no tool result and whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind. A queued_command attachment whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind; one with no origin whose commandMode is set and is not prompt: kind its commandMode. A user line marked isCompactSummary: kind compact_summary. A user line that is no tool result, marked isSidechain, with no parentUuid and no origin, the instruction an agent gave its subagent: kind subagent. source: its origin.from, else its origin.producer, else its origin.name, else NONE. prompt_id: its promptId. text: whole, a user line's string content or its text blocks joined by a newline, a queued command's prompt or its text parts joined by a newline. chars: the characters of text. Any other user line that is no tool result and no prompt feeds no table, unless step 16 makes it a notice.
step · what the models said
n
6
A said row per text block of a Claude Code assistant line: position the block's place in its reply, placed as a thinking span is placed today; text whole; chars its characters. A Gemini reply is a said row, position 0, each time a write of it holds a content longer than its newest said row, superseded writes included.
step · the reply
n
7
A reply takes the columns the structure added, from the line it is written from: request, the request row whose request_key is the line's requestRef, NONE where the line has none or the crawler holds no such request; prompt_id, the newest promptId of the session's lines read before it, a session first read since the crawler began taking its newest from its line rows; client_version its version; skill, plugin, mcp_server and mcp_tool its attributionSkill, attributionPlugin, attributionMcpServer and attributionMcpTool; refusal and refusal_text its message.stop_details.category and message.stop_details.explanation where message.stop_details.type is refusal; cache_miss and cache_missed its message.diagnostics.cache_miss_reason.type and cache_missed_input_tokens. A reply_state takes cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens from its usage's cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens and ephemeral_5m_input_tokens. A Claude Code line whose model is <synthetic> stays no reply, as today (step 16).
step · the reply read again
n
8
A reply read again from its middle (state.rs hold) reads the session's lines from the reply's first line to the line being read, that one included, keeps those of the reply and steps over every other, a subagent's lines among them; it no longer stops at the first line that is not the reply's.
step · calls and results
n
9
A tool call takes skill (a Skill call's input.skill, a Gemini activate_skill call's args.name), model (an Agent call's input.model) and timeout_ms (its input.timeout). A result takes, from its line, when the line carries that result alone, as today's sandbox hosts are read: decision, decided_by, decision_source and decision_reason from permissionDecision.decision, reasonType, source and reasonCode; denial from toolDenialKind; operator_feedback from userFeedback; sandboxed from sandboxed. From its toolUseResult, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task: its persistedOutputSize, returnCodeInterpretation and backgroundTaskId. text: what the tool returned, as text as today's results read it, when its outcome is not ok. A shell command's result that ended ok, whose toolUseResult carries a bashEditDiff not marked shared, is an edit row per entry of its files: path its filePath; kind created where it is marked created, deleted where it is marked deleted, else modified; added and removed the lines of its hunks that begin with + and with -; counted_from shell; operator_changed false. A diff marked shared saw files change while other commands ran too, so the call alone did not change them, and it is no edit row.
step · the requests
n
10
An api-request line is a request row: request_key its id; model, effort, max_tokens, thinking and thinking_display its params.model, params.output_config.effort, params.max_tokens, params.thinking.type and params.thinking.display; betas its params.betas; context_edits the type of each of its params.context_management.edits; query_source its querySource; cache_breakpoints the number of its cacheControl entries; keep and tail its keep and tail; shape_hash its shapeHash; digest its requestDigest; params its params whole. An api-request-blob line is a request_part row, one per hash ever: role its message.role, NONE where it has none; blocks the type of each block of its message.content, text where the content is a string; chars the characters of the text it carries: a string content, a text block's text, a thinking block's thinking, a tool_addition block's tool.definition.description. An api-request-shape line is a request_shape row, one per shapeHash ever: system_parts the number of its shape.system entries, system_chars the characters of their text, tools the name of each of its shape.tools.
step · the context
n
11
Every attachment line but a queued_command is a context row: kind its attachment's type; names, removed and failed empty, changed and reason and tokens_left NONE, chars 0, but as follows. instructions: names the path of each of its files, chars their content, changed and reason its own. skill_listing: names its names, chars its content. invoked_skills: names the name of each of its skills, chars their content. deferred_tools_delta: names its addedNames, readdedNames and surfacedNames, removed its removedNames and the name of each of its retractedTools, failed the name of each of its failedMcpServers, chars its addedLines and the description of each of its surfacedDefinitions' definition. deferred_tools_record: names the name of each of its entries, chars their description. mcp_instructions_delta: names its addedNames, removed its removedNames, chars its addedBlocks. agent_listing_delta: names its addedTypes, removed its removedTypes, chars its addedLines. file: names its filename, chars its content.file.content. edited_text_file: names its filename, chars its snippet. compact_file_reference: names its filename. environment: names the field of each of its changes. date: names its date, changed its own. model: names its identity.modelId, chars its text. prompt_snapshot: names the name of each of its tools, chars each of its systemPrompt entries and each tool's description. hook_additional_context and hook_success: names its hookName, chars its content or its stdout. plan_file_reference: names its planFilePath, chars its planContent. plan_mode and plan_mode_exit: names its planFilePath. task_status: names its taskId, chars its description. command_permissions: names its allowedTools. remote_session_change: names its pr and its commit, each where set. session_context: chars its context.gitStatus and context.userEmail. total_tokens_reminder: tokens_left the number its text gives before " tokens left", chars its text. Every other type: chars its text, content or banner, where it has one that is a string. chars counts characters.
step · the hooks
n
12
A system/stop_hook_summary line is a hook_run row per entry of its hookInfos, position the entry's place: event Stop, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors the number of the line's hookErrors, prevented the line's preventedContinuation, chars 0. A hook_additional_context attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command and ms NONE, errors 0, prevented false, chars its content. A hook_success attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors 0, prevented false, chars its stdout.
step · the session
n
13
An ai-title, permission-mode or mode line is a session_state row when it changes the session's newest title (its aiTitle), permission mode (its permissionMode) or mode (its mode): the row holds the three as they stand after it. A cost-state line is a session_cost row when its figures differ from the session's newest: usd its totalCostUSD; api_ms and api_ms_no_retries its totalAPIDuration and totalAPIDurationWithoutRetries; tool_ms its totalToolDuration; wall_ms its totalDuration; lines_added and lines_removed its totalLinesAdded and totalLinesRemoved; unknown_cost its hasUnknownModelCost. With it, a model_cost row per key of its modelUsage whose entry holds the figures: model the key; usd, input, output, cache_read, cache_write, thinking and web_searches the entry's costUSD, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadInputTokens, cacheCreationInputTokens, thinkingTokens and webSearchRequests. An entry that holds none of them is no row; one that holds some and not others leaves the line unread.
step · the links
n
14
A pr-link line is a session_link of kind pr: url its prUrl, number its prNumber, repository its prRepository. A frame-link line that holds a frameUrl is one of kind artifact: url its frameUrl, title its title. A result of a call to a tool whose name ends createJiraIssue, editJiraIssue or transitionJiraIssue is one of kind issue per entry of its line's mcpMeta.structuredContent.issues.nodes: url the entry's self, key its key, title its fields.summary. One row per session, kind and url ever, first_at and kernel_message the first line that names it.
step · the git state
n
15
A result line's serverClassifierContext.context.git_state that carries no error is a git_state row when it differs from the session's newest: cwd, root, branch and default_branch its own; clean its status.clean; modified and staged its status.counts.modified and status.counts.staged; untracked its status.counts.untracked_normal; remote_host, remote and visibility its visibility.origin.host, visibility.origin.remote and visibility.origin.visibility. One that carries an error holds no state yet, and is no row.
step · the notices
n
16
A notice row for: a Claude Code assistant line marked isApiErrorMessage, kind its error, status its apiErrorStatus, text the text of its content, limit its quotaLimits.rateLimitType, resets_at its quotaLimits.resetsAt, a count of seconds since the Unix epoch, as a time; a system/model_refusal_no_fallback line, kind model_refusal_no_fallback, model its originalModel, text its apiRefusalExplanation; a system/informational line, kind informational, text its content; a system/local_command line, and a user line whose content is a string that begins <local-command-stdout>, kind local_command, text its content; a Gemini info, error or warning line, kind its type, text its content. Every column a line does not give is NONE.
step · the agent's own tools
n
17
Each of these is read when the call's result is: at the call's at. An AskUserQuestion call is a question row per entry of its input's questions, position the entry's place: header, question, multi its multiSelect, options the label of each of its options, answer what its result's answers give for that question, note the notes its result's annotations give for it, each NONE where the result gives none. A Gemini ask_user call is the same per entry of its args.questions, multi its multiSelect, false where it has none, answer what its result's output gives under the question's place in its answers, note NONE. An ExitPlanMode call is a plan row: text its input's plan, chars its characters, file its input's planFilePath, edited whether its result is an object whose plan differs from its input's. A TaskCreate call whose result ended ok is a task_event row, action created, task_key its result's task.id, subject its input's subject, status NONE, text its input's description; a TaskUpdate call whose result ended ok is one with action updated, task_key its input's taskId, subject, status and text its input's subject, status and description; a Gemini update_topic call that ended ok is one with action topic, task_key and status NONE, subject its args.title, text its args.summary and args.strategic_intent joined by a newline. An Agent call whose toolUseResult is an object is a subagent_run row: agent_key its agentId, agent_type its agentType else its call's input.subagent_type, requested_model its call's input.model, model its resolvedModel, status its status, background its isAsync, false where it has none, ms its totalDurationMs, tokens its totalTokens, tool_calls its totalToolUseCount, tool_stats its toolStats, usage its usage, each NONE where it has none. A queue-operation line is a queue_event row: operation and reason its own, chars the characters of its content, 0 where it has none.
step · the check
n
18
For every page it reads, the pass counts with count() the rows the kernel holds over the same ids, the page's first to its last: message rows on a page of them, and on a page of the telemetry stream the rows whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event. Their sum is the source's lines_held; items stays the lines it read. When they differ the source's outcome is partial, its note naming both. pass_source.counts gains lines_held; lines_unused, the line rows the pass wrote whose read_as is empty; fields_seen, the fields (agent, kind, path) its lines showed; fields_new, those it wrote a first line_field row for; and fields_not_stored, those of fields_seen that no column holds. status prints the lines source's line as `<items> read of <lines_held> held, <lines_unused> fed no table, <unread> in a shape it does not know · <fields_seen> fields, <fields_new> new, <fields_not_stored> not stored · <rows> rows`, its outcome after it as today; every other source's line stays as it is.
tests
path
crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/
how
cargo test, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine, the kernel's schema and the structure applied as the merged tests apply them. Lines are written into the kernel as each adapter writes them: message rows with raw and raw_text, telemetry rows with their payloads; and, where an item says so, in the earlier shapes step 1 names. Every line is a fixture of the test's own in the shapes this file names, never a copy of a real line. A format's own rules are tested in its tests module; what crosses the crawler is tested in tests/crawler.rs, a pass run on the kernel and its rows read back. One test per item below, named by the item it proves; no item without its test; no test outside this list and the tests t01 left: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test.
prove
step
1
a pass reads every message row and every transcript_event, transcript_raw and desktop_event row in id order, a side file's content included, and no other telemetry row
prove
step
1
a line kept as its raw_text alone, as its parsed copy alone and as both is read through the same steps into the same rows, for a message row and a transcript_event of each agent; where it is kept as both and the two differ, its rows hold the raw_text's values
prove
step
1
a Gemini superseded write is read as the record it holds, in its place among the lines
prove
step
1
a line of each kind step 1 names as feeding no table is read and counted read; a line of a kind no format knows and a line that is not JSON are counted unread
prove
step
2
every line read is one line row with its kind as step 2 names it, for each agent and each kind of kernel row; a line read again writes no second row
prove
step
2
a line's bytes: its raw_text's where the kernel kept one, a side file's own bytes, a Desktop record's text, 0 for a line kept by its kind alone, else its compact JSON's
prove
step
2
a line's read_as names the tables its read fed, each once, in the structure's order, and is empty for a line that fed none
prove
step
2
a line whose session the kernel names only after its first read keeps the line row of its first read, and a later read writes the facts the first could not
prove
step
3
a path names content blocks by their type, a tool call and its result and toolUseResult by the tool's name, a list's items by [], and a result whose call the crawler does not hold by ?
prove
step
3
a key that is not a name, a UUID, and each key of each map step 3 names is written <k>, and a name elsewhere is not
prove
step
3
a field is a line_field row the first time its agent, kind and path are seen and never again while stored stands, and again when stored changes
prove
step
3
every path a format declares stored lands in a column: a line of each kind the format reads, each declared path holding a value of its own, each value found in a column of the rows that line fed
prove
step
4
a prompt takes its prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index, each NONE where absent
prove
step
5
a task notification, a peer, a coordinator and an auto-continuation on a user line, a queued command marked by its origin and one marked by its commandMode alone, a compact summary and a subagent's first line are each a trigger with its kind, source and text; a meta line with no origin, a line of a shell command's output and a queued command whose commandMode is prompt are none
prove
step
6
a Claude Code text block is one said row at its place; a Gemini reply is one said row per growth of its content, its superseded writes included
prove
step
7
a reply takes its request, prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed, and a reply_state its cache writes by duration; a reply whose requestRef names no request held has request NONE
prove
step
8
a reply read again from its middle, with a subagent's lines between its own, counts each of its lines once and places its blocks as when read in one pass
prove
step
9
a call takes skill, model and timeout_ms; a result takes the permission decision's four columns, its denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task, and, when it did not end ok, its text
prove
step
9
a shell command's bashEditDiff is one edit per file, created, deleted or modified, its lines counted from its hunks, counted_from shell; a diff marked shared, and one of a command that did not end ok, are none
prove
step
10
an api-request line is a request with every column step 10 names; a blob is one request_part per hash, with its blocks and chars; a shape is one request_shape per hash
prove
step
11
each attachment type step 11 names is a context row with the names, removed, failed, chars, changed, reason and tokens_left step 11 gives it; another type counts its text; a queued command is none
prove
step
12
a stop hook summary is one hook_run per hook; a hook's added context and a hook's success are one each
prove
step
13
session_state is written only when the title, permission mode or mode changes; session_cost and its model_cost rows only when the figures change; an empty modelUsage entry is no row, and a partial one leaves the line unread
prove
step
14
a pull request link, an artifact link, and the issues a tracker call created, edited or moved are each one session_link per session, kind and url; a frame-link with no frameUrl is none
prove
step
15
git_state is written only when the git state a result carries differs from the session's newest, and one that carries an error is none
prove
step
16
each kind of notice step 16 names is one notice row with its kind, status, model, text, limit and resets_at
prove
step
17
a question per question asked, from Claude Code and Gemini, with its answer and note or NONE; a plan, edited or not; a task created, updated and a topic; a subagent run with its figures or NONE; a queue change
prove
step
18
a pass whose pages' counts equal the lines it read is complete; one that read fewer is partial, its note naming both
prove
step
18
pass_source.counts holds lines_held, lines_unused, fields_seen, fields_new and fields_not_stored, and status prints them on the lines source's line as step 18 words them
agent-decides
The names and the decomposition of the private functions and types inside the crawler and its tests; the means of each step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies; the contents of the test fixtures within the items the tests element lists; the granularity of the commits within the root's commits step. Nothing else: no table, column, kind, path rule, count name or status word other than this file's and the structure's; no new dependency; no number; no default; no test the list lacks. What is not here and not in the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code is a stop (root method silence).
gates
Local gates run in the checkout, from the repository root. The live gates run after the operator has deployed the branch's head with every step of the root's method deploy, the provision included: the structure is unchanged, but the rows t01's build wrote lack what this task derives, and no row is ever changed, so the database is rebuilt and every line crawled again (rules derived-and-rebuilt). The first pass then reads every line the kernel holds, which takes as long as it takes. The agent asks for the deploy with every value filled, waits for the operator's word, and runs the live gates' commands only.

  <start>Pointed at this file, you are told nothing else. This line must be the first message of a session opened for this build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin, with no other branch checked out and no worktree of an earlier build checked out in the build folder; if your session holds anything before it beyond what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set your model or effort, with its output), such as a conversation, the writing of this spec or another build, stop here, say so, and build nothing, unless the operator named this session to build this task (rules.xml kickoff), and then build on the operator's word. Your label is your own, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: your model's name and the effort you run at, as your session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; when your session does not report your effort, ask the operator for it before you cut the branch. Read whole, in the order the root's method read gives. Then run python3 tools/skill_audit.py on the head of main and compare what it names with what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head: one fewer is a file the audit did not read, since it reads none on a path through a folder named worktrees, and one more is a file git does not hold; either is a stop: say so and build nothing (gate G3). Cut the branch feat/537-lines-v1-&lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt; from the head of main. Every commit message starts with #537 and carries no attribution trailer. Build what this file says and run its local gates; ask the operator for the deploy its live gates need (root method deploy, this file's gates note) and run them on the operator's word; push the branch and open the PR into main with gh: the title "#537 lines v1 · &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;", the body the root's method pr step lists, naming the root's version and this file's version attribute. What this file, the root, the rules and the merged code do not say is a stop, not a guess.</start>

  <piece>The lines task. The crawler crawls every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, side files and lines that are not JSON included, in depth. Each line is a line row naming the tables it fed. Each field a kind of line carries is a line_field row saying whether a column holds it. Every table the structure gives the lines is filled from the shapes the lines are on record in, and the tables the first crawler fills gain the columns the structure added to them. A reply read again steps over the lines between its own. Every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same ids. Nothing of the repositories, their mirrors or their hosts is crawled here: that is t03.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, on the operator's instance superx-recon running on superx/recon. Its crawler does what superx-performance's did. It reads the kernel's message rows and, of its telemetry, Claude Code's system lines turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary and its queued_command attachments. It writes session; session_place; prompt, all but prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index; reply, all but request, prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed; reply_state, all but cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens; thinking; tool_call, all but skill, model and timeout_ms; tool_result, all but decision, decided_by, decision_source, decision_reason, denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note, background_task and text; edit, from the file tools alone; git_event, test_run, build_run, turn, compaction and away. And it lists the repositories. Every other table of the lines is empty.</before>

  <after>Who uses what this task builds: t03 (#538), which follows the sessions' places, git events, git states and links to the repositories and their hosts, and ties commits to sessions; t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table this task fills.</after>

  <reflects>
    <file path="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" keys="tables 1 to 15 and 33 to 53">

      <quote>lists every field the crawler does not store, so none is left out unseen.</quote>

    </file>

  <reads>
    <code path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/">Every file, whole: the crawler as t01 left it.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/status.rs">The status, and the line it prints for a source.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/">The tests that cross the crawler, whole.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs">What a Claude Code line is in the kernel: a user or assistant line, a message row, raw and raw_text; every other line, a transcript_event whose payload holds kind, session, file, line and raw_text; a line that is not JSON, a transcript_raw whose payload holds file, session, snippet, text and raw_text; a side file, a transcript_event of kind file whose line holds type, path, bytes, modified and content, and no raw_text.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/gemini_cli.rs">What a Gemini CLI line is in the kernel: a user or gemini record, a message row; a header, a $set, an info, error or warning line, and an earlier write of a record (kind superseded), a transcript_event; a line of a shape it does not know, a transcript_raw whose payload holds its line.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_desktop.rs">What a Claude Desktop record is in the kernel: a desktop_event whose payload holds the record's text as line, and file.</code>
    <code path="crates/superx-kernel/src/message.rs">message rows: raw, and raw_text.</code>
  </reads>

  <command>
    <form>superx-recon run, as t01 left it: the crawler's passes, one every attr_recon_read_secs.</form>
    <runs>Its own process, as today. After the provision of this task's deploy, the first pass crawls every line the kernel holds.</runs>
  </command>

  <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/" name="the lines part of the crawler">
      <step n="1" name="every line">Each pass reads, in the order of their ids as today and in pages as bounded as today's, every message row, its raw_text beside its raw, and every telemetry_stream row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event, its payload whole, a side file's content included; every other telemetry row is no line and is skipped, as today. A line is read from its raw_text, parsed as JSON, wherever the kernel kept one, and from its parsed copy, a message row's raw or a payload's line, only where the kernel kept no raw_text: the kernel kept the parsed copy alone before #529, both from #529, the raw_text alone while #542 was its build, and both again from #546, and a line kept in any of these shapes goes through the same steps below. A side file and a Claude Desktop record are read from their payload's line, as today. A Gemini transcript_event of kind superseded holds an earlier write of a user or gemini record, whole, and is read as that record is read, in its place among the lines. Each agent's format knows the kinds of line the steps below read, and these, which feed no table: Claude Code's file-history-snapshot, file-history-delta, last-prompt, atis-latch, agent-name, fork-context-ref, started, launched, result, failed, artifact-autoreact-ledger and artifact-comment-monitor lines and its side files; Gemini's header and $set lines; every Claude Desktop record. A line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON, and a line in which a format finds a shape it does not read, as today, is counted unread.</step>
      <step n="2" name="the line">Every line read is one line row, written once ever: when the line is first read, as that read found it, and after every other row that read wrote from it. For each page it reads, the crawler asks its database which of the page's kernel ids line already holds; a line read again writes no line row, though it may write a fact its first read could not, such as one of a session the kernel has named since. kernel_message: its id. at: as today, the line's own time where it has one, else when the kernel captured it. agent: the agent's name the kernel gives it; a line the kernel names no agent for is no line row, and is counted unread. session: the session row of the session the kernel names for it, NONE where it names none, as for a Claude Desktop record or a project's own side file. kind: the line's type; for a Claude Code system line its type, a slash and its subtype, and for an attachment its type, a slash and its attachment's type; where the line holds no type, the kind the kernel gave it (a Gemini header or $set, a line kept by its kind alone before #471); a Claude Code line that is not JSON, transcript_raw; a Claude Desktop record, desktop_event. agent_uuid: its uuid. parent_uuid: its parentUuid, else its logicalParentUuid. prompt_id: its promptId. sidechain: its isSidechain, false where it has none. client_version: its version. bytes: the length in bytes of the line's raw_text, where the kernel kept one; for a side file, the bytes its line gives; for a Claude Desktop record, the length in bytes of its text; for a line kept by its kind alone, 0; for a line captured before the kernel kept raw_text, the length of the line as captured written out as compact JSON (value::json_len). read_as: the tables this read wrote a row to from the line, each once, in the order the structure's read_as list gives them; empty when it wrote none.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the fields">Every field of every line that is JSON is counted by its path. Each (agent, kind, path) is one line_field row the first time a line shows it, first_at the line's at, first_line its kernel id, stored whether a column holds it; and one row more each time stored changes for it. The crawler holds the newest row of each, read from its database at its first pass, and writes one only when it differs. A path is the keys from the line's top to the field, joined by a dot. A list's items take the list's path with [] after it. A block of a message's content list is named by its type in brackets, message.content[text]; a tool call by its name, message.content[tool_use:Bash]; a tool result by the name of the call it answers, message.content[tool_result:Bash], with ? where the crawler holds no such call; a line's toolUseResult the same way, toolUseResult[Bash]. A key is written &lt;k&gt; when it is data: a key that is not a name, a name being made of ASCII letters, digits, _, $ and - and beginning with a letter, _ or $; a key that is a UUID; and every key of a map the agent's format names as keyed by data, for Claude Code modelUsage, snapshot.trackedFileBackups, artifacts, wireToolInputs and wireIngestContext, the answers and the annotations of an AskUserQuestion result, and the files of an Artifact call's input. Each agent's format declares, per kind, the paths whose values its reading puts in a column, a path whose value a column holds whole standing for itself and every path under it; those paths are stored, and every other is not.</step>
      <step n="4" name="the operator's prompts">A prompt is read as today, and takes the columns the structure added: prompt_id its promptId, source its promptSource, permission_mode its permissionMode, turn_index and prompt_index its turnPosition.turnIndex and turnPosition.promptIndex, each NONE where the line has none.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the triggers">A line that is no prompt as today's prompts are read is a trigger row when the first of these holds. A user line that is no tool result and whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind. A queued_command attachment whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind; one with no origin whose commandMode is set and is not prompt: kind its commandMode. A user line marked isCompactSummary: kind compact_summary. A user line that is no tool result, marked isSidechain, with no parentUuid and no origin, the instruction an agent gave its subagent: kind subagent. source: its origin.from, else its origin.producer, else its origin.name, else NONE. prompt_id: its promptId. text: whole, a user line's string content or its text blocks joined by a newline, a queued command's prompt or its text parts joined by a newline. chars: the characters of text. Any other user line that is no tool result and no prompt feeds no table, unless step 16 makes it a notice.</step>
      <step n="6" name="what the models said">A said row per text block of a Claude Code assistant line: position the block's place in its reply, placed as a thinking span is placed today; text whole; chars its characters. A Gemini reply is a said row, position 0, each time a write of it holds a content longer than its newest said row, superseded writes included.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the reply">A reply takes the columns the structure added, from the line it is written from: request, the request row whose request_key is the line's requestRef, NONE where the line has none or the crawler holds no such request; prompt_id, the newest promptId of the session's lines read before it, a session first read since the crawler began taking its newest from its line rows; client_version its version; skill, plugin, mcp_server and mcp_tool its attributionSkill, attributionPlugin, attributionMcpServer and attributionMcpTool; refusal and refusal_text its message.stop_details.category and message.stop_details.explanation where message.stop_details.type is refusal; cache_miss and cache_missed its message.diagnostics.cache_miss_reason.type and cache_missed_input_tokens. A reply_state takes cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens from its usage's cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens and ephemeral_5m_input_tokens. A Claude Code line whose model is &lt;synthetic&gt; stays no reply, as today (step 16).</step>
      <step n="8" name="the reply read again">A reply read again from its middle (state.rs hold) reads the session's lines from the reply's first line to the line being read, that one included, keeps those of the reply and steps over every other, a subagent's lines among them; it no longer stops at the first line that is not the reply's.</step>
      <step n="9" name="calls and results">A tool call takes skill (a Skill call's input.skill, a Gemini activate_skill call's args.name), model (an Agent call's input.model) and timeout_ms (its input.timeout). A result takes, from its line, when the line carries that result alone, as today's sandbox hosts are read: decision, decided_by, decision_source and decision_reason from permissionDecision.decision, reasonType, source and reasonCode; denial from toolDenialKind; operator_feedback from userFeedback; sandboxed from sandboxed. From its toolUseResult, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task: its persistedOutputSize, returnCodeInterpretation and backgroundTaskId. text: what the tool returned, as text as today's results read it, when its outcome is not ok. A shell command's result that ended ok, whose toolUseResult carries a bashEditDiff not marked shared, is an edit row per entry of its files: path its filePath; kind created where it is marked created, deleted where it is marked deleted, else modified; added and removed the lines of its hunks that begin with + and with -; counted_from shell; operator_changed false. A diff marked shared saw files change while other commands ran too, so the call alone did not change them, and it is no edit row.</step>
      <step n="10" name="the requests">An api-request line is a request row: request_key its id; model, effort, max_tokens, thinking and thinking_display its params.model, params.output_config.effort, params.max_tokens, params.thinking.type and params.thinking.display; betas its params.betas; context_edits the type of each of its params.context_management.edits; query_source its querySource; cache_breakpoints the number of its cacheControl entries; keep and tail its keep and tail; shape_hash its shapeHash; digest its requestDigest; params its params whole. An api-request-blob line is a request_part row, one per hash ever: role its message.role, NONE where it has none; blocks the type of each block of its message.content, text where the content is a string; chars the characters of the text it carries: a string content, a text block's text, a thinking block's thinking, a tool_addition block's tool.definition.description. An api-request-shape line is a request_shape row, one per shapeHash ever: system_parts the number of its shape.system entries, system_chars the characters of their text, tools the name of each of its shape.tools.</step>
      <step n="11" name="the context">Every attachment line but a queued_command is a context row: kind its attachment's type; names, removed and failed empty, changed and reason and tokens_left NONE, chars 0, but as follows. instructions: names the path of each of its files, chars their content, changed and reason its own. skill_listing: names its names, chars its content. invoked_skills: names the name of each of its skills, chars their content. deferred_tools_delta: names its addedNames, readdedNames and surfacedNames, removed its removedNames and the name of each of its retractedTools, failed the name of each of its failedMcpServers, chars its addedLines and the description of each of its surfacedDefinitions' definition. deferred_tools_record: names the name of each of its entries, chars their description. mcp_instructions_delta: names its addedNames, removed its removedNames, chars its addedBlocks. agent_listing_delta: names its addedTypes, removed its removedTypes, chars its addedLines. file: names its filename, chars its content.file.content. edited_text_file: names its filename, chars its snippet. compact_file_reference: names its filename. environment: names the field of each of its changes. date: names its date, changed its own. model: names its identity.modelId, chars its text. prompt_snapshot: names the name of each of its tools, chars each of its systemPrompt entries and each tool's description. hook_additional_context and hook_success: names its hookName, chars its content or its stdout. plan_file_reference: names its planFilePath, chars its planContent. plan_mode and plan_mode_exit: names its planFilePath. task_status: names its taskId, chars its description. command_permissions: names its allowedTools. remote_session_change: names its pr and its commit, each where set. session_context: chars its context.gitStatus and context.userEmail. total_tokens_reminder: tokens_left the number its text gives before " tokens left", chars its text. Every other type: chars its text, content or banner, where it has one that is a string. chars counts characters.</step>
      <step n="12" name="the hooks">A system/stop_hook_summary line is a hook_run row per entry of its hookInfos, position the entry's place: event Stop, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors the number of the line's hookErrors, prevented the line's preventedContinuation, chars 0. A hook_additional_context attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command and ms NONE, errors 0, prevented false, chars its content. A hook_success attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors 0, prevented false, chars its stdout.</step>
      <step n="13" name="the session">An ai-title, permission-mode or mode line is a session_state row when it changes the session's newest title (its aiTitle), permission mode (its permissionMode) or mode (its mode): the row holds the three as they stand after it. A cost-state line is a session_cost row when its figures differ from the session's newest: usd its totalCostUSD; api_ms and api_ms_no_retries its totalAPIDuration and totalAPIDurationWithoutRetries; tool_ms its totalToolDuration; wall_ms its totalDuration; lines_added and lines_removed its totalLinesAdded and totalLinesRemoved; unknown_cost its hasUnknownModelCost. With it, a model_cost row per key of its modelUsage whose entry holds the figures: model the key; usd, input, output, cache_read, cache_write, thinking and web_searches the entry's costUSD, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadInputTokens, cacheCreationInputTokens, thinkingTokens and webSearchRequests. An entry that holds none of them is no row; one that holds some and not others leaves the line unread.</step>
      <step n="14" name="the links">A pr-link line is a session_link of kind pr: url its prUrl, number its prNumber, repository its prRepository. A frame-link line that holds a frameUrl is one of kind artifact: url its frameUrl, title its title. A result of a call to a tool whose name ends createJiraIssue, editJiraIssue or transitionJiraIssue is one of kind issue per entry of its line's mcpMeta.structuredContent.issues.nodes: url the entry's self, key its key, title its fields.summary. One row per session, kind and url ever, first_at and kernel_message the first line that names it.</step>
      <step n="15" name="the git state">A result line's serverClassifierContext.context.git_state that carries no error is a git_state row when it differs from the session's newest: cwd, root, branch and default_branch its own; clean its status.clean; modified and staged its status.counts.modified and status.counts.staged; untracked its status.counts.untracked_normal; remote_host, remote and visibility its visibility.origin.host, visibility.origin.remote and visibility.origin.visibility. One that carries an error holds no state yet, and is no row.</step>
      <step n="16" name="the notices">A notice row for: a Claude Code assistant line marked isApiErrorMessage, kind its error, status its apiErrorStatus, text the text of its content, limit its quotaLimits.rateLimitType, resets_at its quotaLimits.resetsAt, a count of seconds since the Unix epoch, as a time; a system/model_refusal_no_fallback line, kind model_refusal_no_fallback, model its originalModel, text its apiRefusalExplanation; a system/informational line, kind informational, text its content; a system/local_command line, and a user line whose content is a string that begins &lt;local-command-stdout&gt;, kind local_command, text its content; a Gemini info, error or warning line, kind its type, text its content. Every column a line does not give is NONE.</step>
      <step n="17" name="the agent's own tools">Each of these is read when the call's result is: at the call's at. An AskUserQuestion call is a question row per entry of its input's questions, position the entry's place: header, question, multi its multiSelect, options the label of each of its options, answer what its result's answers give for that question, note the notes its result's annotations give for it, each NONE where the result gives none. A Gemini ask_user call is the same per entry of its args.questions, multi its multiSelect, false where it has none, answer what its result's output gives under the question's place in its answers, note NONE. An ExitPlanMode call is a plan row: text its input's plan, chars its characters, file its input's planFilePath, edited whether its result is an object whose plan differs from its input's. A TaskCreate call whose result ended ok is a task_event row, action created, task_key its result's task.id, subject its input's subject, status NONE, text its input's description; a TaskUpdate call whose result ended ok is one with action updated, task_key its input's taskId, subject, status and text its input's subject, status and description; a Gemini update_topic call that ended ok is one with action topic, task_key and status NONE, subject its args.title, text its args.summary and args.strategic_intent joined by a newline. An Agent call whose toolUseResult is an object is a subagent_run row: agent_key its agentId, agent_type its agentType else its call's input.subagent_type, requested_model its call's input.model, model its resolvedModel, status its status, background its isAsync, false where it has none, ms its totalDurationMs, tokens its totalTokens, tool_calls its totalToolUseCount, tool_stats its toolStats, usage its usage, each NONE where it has none. A queue-operation line is a queue_event row: operation and reason its own, chars the characters of its content, 0 where it has none.</step>
      <step n="18" name="the check">For every page it reads, the pass counts with count() the rows the kernel holds over the same ids, the page's first to its last: message rows on a page of them, and on a page of the telemetry stream the rows whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event. Their sum is the source's lines_held; items stays the lines it read. When they differ the source's outcome is partial, its note naming both. pass_source.counts gains lines_held; lines_unused, the line rows the pass wrote whose read_as is empty; fields_seen, the fields (agent, kind, path) its lines showed; fields_new, those it wrote a first line_field row for; and fields_not_stored, those of fields_seen that no column holds. status prints the lines source's line as `&lt;items&gt; read of &lt;lines_held&gt; held, &lt;lines_unused&gt; fed no table, &lt;unread&gt; in a shape it does not know · &lt;fields_seen&gt; fields, &lt;fields_new&gt; new, &lt;fields_not_stored&gt; not stored · &lt;rows&gt; rows`, its outcome after it as today; every other source's line stays as it is.</step>
    </module>

    <tests path="crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/">

The crawler crawls the repositories and their hosts. From the working directories the lines name, and the roots the operator names, every checkout and repository; one worker per repository, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows. For each repository its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, and every fact git holds: commits with their files, renames and copies, their time zones, patch ids, signatures and trailers; how each merge's conflicts were resolved; annotated tags; refs with their upstreams and how far each is ahead and behind, and every reflog entry; HEAD moves and operations in progress; where each commit sits and what landed through another commit, what each commit still holds at the main line's tip, the files at the tip, uncommitted work, remotes and set-up, and every git call. From the hosts, with the credentials and GET only, what the hosts' tables hold. Every commit tied to the session that made it.
It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.

BLUEPRINT.md 186 lines · the architecture
# SuperX Blueprint — The Agentic OS (Reset of 2026-08-06)

> **Status: v1.0 — decisions D1–D4 locked by the operator 2026-08-06.**
> This document lands with the G0 reset PR; merging that PR is the
> operator's approval of the reset. The complete v1 FVP is preserved at
> the tag `archive/pre-reset-2026-08-06`. This is the living canon —
> it replaces `ARCHITECTURE.md` (retired with v1).

## 1. Mission

SuperX is an **agentic operating system** written in Rust.

- **SurrealDB is the OS's filesystem.** Every fact the OS knows —
  agents, telemetry, state, configuration, the module registry itself —
  lives in the substrate. If it isn't in SurrealDB, the OS doesn't know
  it. No sidecar config files, no in-memory-only state that matters —
  with one deliberately narrow bootstrap-scope exception (D12/D16):
  what must be known BEFORE the substrate is reachable lives in the
  `<home>/{params,logs,db}` instance layout (`params/superx.json`,
  the credentials file, the pidfile, the logs).
- **Telemetry capture is the kernel's core capability.** From the moment
  the OS boots, it captures all telemetry emitted by every agent on the
  system. This is not a module, not an option, not a plugin — it is
  what the kernel *is*. An agentic OS that cannot see its agents is not
  an OS.
- **Everything else is a module.** Data fusion, graphify, and every
  future capability arrive as modules loaded on top of the kernel. The
  kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain.

## 2. What the kernel does (and nothing else)

1. **Substrate access** — typed verbs over SurrealDB. Append-only:
   SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL.
   "Current state" is computed, never mutated (SCD-2).
2. **Boot** — `superx boot` connects to the substrate, registers
   modules, brings them up with failure isolation, and starts the
   telemetry engine. Boot always completes; a failed module never
   takes the OS down.
3. **Agent telemetry capture** — the engine that discovers agents on
   the machine, tails whatever they emit, writes typed telemetry rows
   **and first-class conversation rows** (`message` table — every
   session with every agent, very detailed, raw event preserved), and
   checkpoints with cursors so restarts are lossless. Runs from boot
   until shutdown; captures everything by default (operator decision
   2026-08-07 — no per-agent grant gate; OS-level file prompts are the
   permission surface).
4. **CLI** — the operator interface: `--initialize` (one-command
   provisioning + background boot, idempotent per instance; the
   terminal returns), `start` / `stop` / `restart` / `status` (background-OS
   lifecycle, pidfile-backed), `agents`, `actions [--agent] [--live]` (the
   telemetry stream), `sessions` (list conversations), `read
   <session-fragment> [--live]` (render a conversation, historical
   then follow), `logs [--follow] [--daemon]` (the OS's own log).
   `boot` remains as the foreground debug mode. All bootstrap config
   resolves flag > env > `params/superx.json` > fallback. The CLI is
   part of the kernel deliverable, not an app bolted on later.
5. **Module system** — modules register with the kernel, declare
   dependencies, and get lifecycle management (starting / active /
   failed / skipped). The registry lives in the substrate like
   everything else.

**Self-log.** The kernel keeps its own rolling log file in a local
`logs/` directory (override: `SUPERX_LOG_DIR`), independent of the
substrate — boot diagnostics and signin failures must be observable
when the database is down. Substrate telemetry remains the audit log
of record; the self-log is the layer below it. (Operator directive,
2026-08-07.)

**Agent adapters.** The capture *engine* is kernel-core. Knowing how a
*specific* agent emits telemetry (Claude Code writes JSONL transcripts;
another agent may expose OTLP, logs, or an API) is adapter knowledge.
Adapters are the one plugin interface the kernel itself defines — small,
per-agent, and hot-swappable without touching the engine.

## 3. What carries forward from v1 (proven mechanics, not code)

The v1 codebase is archived wholesale; no file survives on `main`. But
these v1 mechanics were verified live and are carried forward as
*design decisions*, re-implemented clean:

- Append-only substrate with SCD-2 (`valid_from`, latest-wins reads).
- Types-as-data metamodel: new entity/attr types arrive by INSERT,
  never by schema migration.
- Cursor-checkpointed capture: poll → emit telemetry → write cursor;
  restart-lossless; errors become telemetry, never panics.
- Service-account discipline: the kernel signs in as a minimal-privilege
  user, never root; the operator owns the schema and applies it once.
- Module lifecycle with failure isolation and topological boot order.
- Tolerant parsing: unknown telemetry shapes are captured raw, not
  dropped.

## 4. What is retired

- The 6-crate L0–L3 layering (kernel / kernel-modules / drivers / apps).
  v2 started as **one workspace, two crates**: `superx-kernel` (lib) and
  `superx` (bin, the CLI). Crates are extracted only when a module
  graduates and proves the seam — the graduates are
  `superx-mod-hello`, the reference module (epic #141), `superx-ops` (P3): the shared runners/renderers both the CLI and
  modules consume — kernel-only dependencies, so modules never depend
  on the bin — and `superx-mod-ui` (P4+): the UI module itself, an
  axum server inside the daemon on `attr_ui_port`: the template
  contributors copy, exercising every facility of the module contract
  (own schema/db, own dir, own log, own CLI).
- The F-phase roadmap, the 2026-06 quality backlog, and all 20 open
  pre-reset GitHub issues (they reference retired concepts).
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` (v42.15, 88 KB) — the historical vision document.
  This blueprint replaces it as the living canon. Bloat was a named
  failure mode; this document stays under 300 lines or it gets split.

## 5. The new FVP

> The operator runs `superx boot`. Every coding agent on the machine
> is discovered and stored in the substrate. Detailed telemetry AND
> full conversations stream in from Claude Code, Gemini CLI, and
> Claude Desktop. `superx actions --live` shows what agents are doing;
> `superx read <session> --live` lets the operator read any
> conversation — historical first, then live as it continues. Adding a
> new agent adapter touches zero engine code.

Measured by: **three** agent adapters working day one (Claude Code,
Gemini CLI, Claude Desktop), conversations readable end-to-end,
lossless restart mid-stream, and the whole workspace green at the
gates.

## 6. Phase plan (each phase = one branch, one PR, one day max)

| # | Scope | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|
| G0 | Reset PR: archive tag, wipe to skeleton, this blueprint, fresh README | `main` = blueprint + empty 2-crate workspace, gates green |
| G1 | Schema design session (operator + model, §11) → `SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` v2 + `schema/kernel.surql` v2; operator applies under root | Schema deployed and locked |
| G2 | Kernel substrate verbs + telemetry primitive + tests | Verbs green on kv-mem |
| G3 | Module system + boot orchestrator | Fake-module boot tests green |

The fault ledger (`superx specs ledger`) sets every spec version a session committed — by which model, and why — beside every build of it and what its git did to the build's branch. It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed. It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture. It replaces `superx-mod-performance` (`superx-performance`), its first build, and `superx-mod-repos` (`superx-repos`), the first repository crawler: their code moves into it and their crates leave the workspace in the spec's first task | `repos` schema operator-approved 2026-10-21; the executable, its registry entry and its URL (#467). `performance` schema operator-approved 2026-10-24; its executable, registry entry and URL (#506); the reader (#508). | **G8** | **`superx-mod-entities` — third attempt** (2026-08-28, epic #353). THREE TABLES: `entity` is a uuid7 and nothing else, `entity_attribute` is everything said about it including its name, `entity_edge` is a native relation. | D12 | Credential persistence (2026-08-18) | Service password saved to a `0600` `superx-credentials` file beside the datastore so query commands need no exports; env var still wins |
| D13 | Git discipline (2026-08-18) | Every change ties to a GitHub issue; branches + PRs reference it |
| D14 | Session lookup (2026-08-18, #122) | Sessions identified `<agent>/<uuid7>`; `read` matches any unique fragment; source id rides as `src=` |
| D15 | Background OS (2026-08-18, #124) | `--initialize` returns the terminal: OS runs detached (pidfile, daemon log); `superx stop`; duplicate-capture guards; shutdown honored between source polls |
| D16 | Instance config (2026-08-18, #125) | `params/superx.json` controls all bootstrap config inside the `<home>/{params,logs,db}` layout; precedence flag > env > file > fallback; `superx logs` surfaces the self-log. Released as **v1.0.0** |
| D17 | Module facility contract (2026-08-19, epic #141) | A module is a self-contained mini-app: own db `superx/<name>` + service account (via `modules provision`), own dir, own log target, own CLI namespace, own parameters; kernel is the hub — modules depend on the kernel only, never on each other |
| D18 | Module identity (2026-08-19, epic #141) | Every module is a registry entity with a uuid7 id; same-kind modules coexist (nothing assumes a singleton); compiled-in v1 contract (linkme), WASM parked |
| D19 | Native graph edges (2026-08-19, epic #166) | The entities module links nodes with SurrealDB `RELATE` edges (`TYPE RELATION IN entity OUT entity ENFORCED`): traversal follows record pointers (cost = a node's degree, never table size); RELATE is creation-only — blessed as CREATE-equivalent under the append-only contract, module db only |
| D20 | Identity/state split (2026-08-19, epic #166) | Graph nodes = one immutable uuid7 anchor row (stable edge target, forever) + SCD-2 `entity_state` version chains; edge history = `edge_uid` chains where unlink appends `active=false` |
| D21 | Executor is the next epic (2026-08-19, epic #166) | This epic ships the graph substrate + CLI; the runner module that walks the graph and executes task nodes — and the dashboard graph page — follow separately |
| D22 | Text-as-entity (2026-08-19, epic #166) | All long-form text (descriptions, comments, instructions) is a `text` NODE linked by role edges (`describes`/`comments`/`instructs`), direction owner → target; entities keep a one-line `name` label; describe/instruct evolve one node (its history = the text's evolution), comments multiply (threads = comment a comment) |
| D23 | Runner schema (2026-08-19, epic #189) | Two tables, SCD-2, inserts only, uuid7 everywhere: `schedule` intent chains (time + entity ref + recurrence — NOTHING else; the graph is the plan) + `run` fact chains (one per firing×task; statuses appended) |
| D24 | Cross-module contract v1 (2026-08-19, epic #189) | Modules talk through each other's CLIs via the kernel's in-process dispatch (`entities graph --json`, `entities create/link`) — the same surface humans, the Console, and agents use; no module-to-module crate deps |
| D25 | Dependency semantics (2026-08-19, epic #189) | `depends_on` edges among task nodes; runnable = all deps done THIS firing; the runnable frontier dispatches in parallel up to `attr_runner_max_parallel`; failed deps block dependents (never dispatched) |
| D26 | Executor v1 (2026-08-19, epic #189) | One contract: spawn `attr_runner_agent_cmd` (NO default — unset refuses loudly) with the assembled prompt (task name · instructs · product describes · 1-hop linked context); stdout writes back as `task —produced→ text`; recurrence `every:<n><s\|m\|h\|d>` |
| D27 | In-flight steering (2026-08-19, epic #189) | The subgraph re-reads at every frontier re-evaluation — mid-run graph/instruction edits apply to everything not yet dispatched; every run row pins the instruct version it dispatched; cancel halts future dispatch, in-flight finishes and records |

## 8. Binding rules (unchanged from v1, restated)

Trunk-based development: single trunk `main`, always releasable, short-
lived branches, one PR at a time, all-or-nothing branches. Gates:
`cargo test --workspace` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets
--all-features -- -D warnings` + `python3 tools/skill_audit.py`
(CI-enforced as required checks). Schema changes are operator-approved,
per-change, schema-first (`SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` before code). The kernel
authenticates as a service account, never root. No hardcoded policy —
constants flow through substrate parameters.

.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md 189 lines · the mandates every change obeys
---
name: zero-trust-execution
description: SuperX project operating mode — invoke (or treat as always-on) for any work in the SuperX codebase. Enforces no shortcuts, mandatory documentation research, fidelity to BLUEPRINT.md, a stop-and-ask protocol, and verification-as-truth (cargo test + cargo clippy -- -D warnings + skill_audit.py).
---

<instructions>
You are operating under **Zero-Trust Execution Mode** for SuperX — the
agentic operating system (reset of 2026-08-06; canon: `BLUEPRINT.md`).
These mandates are binding. The section numbers are load-bearing: CI
(`.github/workflows/skill-audit.yml`) and `tools/skill_audit.py`
reference them by number.

### §1 The Anti-Velocity Mandate
- NEVER optimize for speed. No workarounds, hacks, or quick fixes to
  get past a compiler, database, or runtime error.
- If you hit an error you do not fully understand: **STOP**. Research
  it or ask. Never paper over it.

### §2 The Research Imperative
- Every technical decision (library configuration, data-type handling,
  error semantics) is grounded in official documentation — SurrealDB,
  Tokio, clap, serde — fetched and read, not recalled.
- Never guess a function signature. Never hallucinate a workaround.
  Implement exactly what the library authors specify, in idiomatic Rust.

### §3 Architectural Fidelity
- `BLUEPRINT.md` and the operator's explicit instructions are absolute
  law. You have ZERO authority to alter the architecture, the kernel's
  five responsibilities, the module/adapter seams, or core patterns
  (append-only substrate, SCD-2, cursor checkpointing) without explicit
  prior operator permission.
- The kernel boundary is part of the architecture: the kernel owns
  substrate access, boot, the telemetry capture engine, the CLI, and
  the module system — and knows nothing about any module's domain.
  Agent-format knowledge lives only in adapters. Module or adapter
  logic leaking into the engine is an architecture change and requires
  operator sign-off.
- If an implementation detail seems to conflict with the blueprint:
  **STOP AND ASK.** Do not invent a path forward.

### §4 The Stop-and-Ask Protocol
Halt and ask the operator when any of these holds:
1. An error prevents the workspace from compiling or tests from passing
   and the fix is not evident.
2. Official documentation contradicts your understanding.
3. You are tempted to write a workaround because the correct way seems
   too difficult or slow.
4. You realize you are acting on an assumption rather than a verified
   fact.

### §5 Verification Is the Only Truth
- Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes.
- Code is not clean until
  `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
  passes.
- The tree is not compliant until `python3 tools/skill_audit.py` passes.
- You are forbidden from declaring a task complete, or updating
  `BLUEPRINT.md` status, until all three gates are green.

### §6 Trunk-Based Development (mandatory workflow)
- Single trunk `main`, always releasable. Every commit on `main` passes
  the §5 gates. Releases are tagged from `main`.
- All work happens on a topic branch (`feat/`, `fix/`, `chore/`,
  `docs/`); every change ships as a PR against `main`. Never commit to
  `main` directly; never force-push `main`.
- Every change ties to a GitHub issue (operator directive 2026-08-18);
  branch names carry the issue number and PRs reference/close it.
- Branches are short-lived (hours, never overnight). One open branch +
  one open PR at a time — sequential, never parallel.
- Each branch is atomic: it lands fully working (gates green,
  feature operational) or it does not land. Unfinished work hides
  behind a substrate parameter flag, never as broken code on `main`.
- One logical step per PR — small diffs. If two changes can be
  reasoned about independently, they are two PRs. Load-bearing
  refactors use branch-by-abstraction: seam first, migrate second,
  remove third — each PR green.
- Pre-flight before any branch: is the change self-contained; is the
  path to green gates visible; does the diff fit one mental model; is
  a flag needed; can you state the PR description in 2–3 sentences?
- Banned: mega-branches, speculative branches, long-lived branches,
  force-merging red gates, mid-branch side-quests.
- Merged branches are deleted, locally and on origin.

### §7 Schema Immutability — STOP. ASK. THEN MAYBE.
- The substrate schema (`SUPERX_SCHEMA.md` and every `schema/*.surql`
  file, once G1 lands them) is load-bearing architecture. Every table,
  field, index, and permission is a contract.
- You have ZERO authority to change schema-defining files without
  explicit, prior, **per-change** operator permission. "Refactor,"
  "cleanup," "small change," and "the prior PR was wrong" are not
  authorization. Authorization for one change does not extend to
  adjacent changes.
- CI enforces this: any PR touching schema-defining files or
  `.claude/skills/` fails unless the PR body carries an
  `Operator-approved: …` marker.
