        .lines()
        .map(str::to_string)
        .collect();
    Ok((lines, (last_newline + 1) as u64))
}

    <text>Recon is one module: its own executable, superx-recon; its own process, with its pid file and its logs in its module folder; its own database, superx/recon, applied by superx-recon --initialize; its own port and tab; its own parameters on its registry entity. It depends on superx-kernel alone, and no crate of the workspace depends on it. The kernel never waits on it: a recon that is stopped, slow or broken leaves capture untouched.</text>

  <rule id="crawl-not-skim">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-25: "crawler means slow diligent methodical"; "we crawl not skim". 2026-10-26: the crawler finds the details that are not readily apparent, and is counted on to deliver critical data no one was aware of</from>
    <text>The crawler crawls. Every field a source carries gets a column or a table, or a line_field row that says no column holds it. Every line read is a line row that names the tables it fed, none when it fed none. Every repository is read whole: all of its history, every ref, every reflog, every blame. Every pass checks what it read against what its source holds, the kernel's lines in the range read, git's own counts, a host's totals, and records any difference; a pass that finds one is partial, the difference in its note. Speed is no goal; pacing is, and capture comes first.</text>

    <text>No sampling, no cap, no truncation, no summary: a limit's fallback is no limit; a value is kept as the source wrote it, in the column the structure gives it; a line, a field or a value the crawler cannot place is counted and named, never dropped and never guessed at.</text>

A line captured before a capture fix is read as it is: the crawler never recaptures, repairs or fills in a kernel line.</text>

A repository no session and no root leads to is never crawled, and one the operator excludes in attr_recon_exclude never is. The lines are crawled as one stream, in the order the kernel captured them. The pass waits for every worker before it records itself.</text>

    <text>Nothing the crawler does slows or stops capture: it reads the kernel's database a bounded page at a time, holds nothing open between pages, and reads nothing while attr_recon_read_secs is 0; a pass that cannot read waits for the next.</text>
    <breach>the live gates, read beside capture's own ticks; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="no-hardcoded-policy">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Facts go in columns; judgments do not."</from>
    <text>Every tunable is a parameter attr_recon_*, with its fallback in the module's params.rs, reported by name when it is set to a value of the wrong shape; a constant that is no policy carries an audited skill-allow marker. Judgments, such as which work the operator directed, what is rework and how work is split into sittings, are parameters applied when a chart is drawn, never columns and never constants.</text>
    <breach>tools/skill_audit.py (§9); review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="types-flow">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "A `record&lt;table&gt;` is a `Thing` everywhere."</from>
    <text>Types flow end to end: a link is a record id, a kernel line's id a Uuid, a time a DateTime&lt;Utc&gt;, from the line to the column; nothing is cast to text to pass the engine, and a refusal of the engine is fixed in the code, never by loosening the structure (§12, §14).</text>
    <breach>tools/skill_audit.py (§14); the structure's own asserts; review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="gates-are-the-truth">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes."</from>
    <text>A task is done when cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings and python3 tools/skill_audit.py pass and every gate of its file is green; nothing is called done, ready or complete before, and a gate that cannot fail is no gate.</text>
    <breach>CI's required checks; the PR's gate table</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="trunk">
    <from>.claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md: "Every change ties to a GitHub issue"</from>
    <text>Each task is one issue, one branch cut from the head of main and one PR into main, merged by the operator; one open branch and one open PR at a time; a merged branch is deleted.</text>
    <breach>review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="specs-are-the-truth">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: "xmls are the source of truth"</from>
    <text>The XML files under specs/recon/ are the source: the issues and the Build Spec page are rendered from them (build_issues.py, build_page.py) and nothing flows back but an issue's number. A spec file is committed only when specs/recon/check.py passes on it, and the check's mutation test proves the check catches what it guards.</text>
    <breach>specs/recon/check.py; specs/recon/mutate_check.py</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="one-agent">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's ruling of 2026-10-03 for every build from a spec</from>
    <text>One session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds a task does every step itself: reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed. This rule is a constraint of every task.</text>
    <breach>review of the PR's history</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="one-build-per-task">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is, the operator's rulings of 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-05 for every build from a spec</from>
    <text>Each version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch, one PR; the operator reviews and merges before the next task starts. The next task's agent reads what the root's method read lists and nothing of an unmerged branch. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes is it built by several agents, one per model, each from the same file version on its own branch; none is merged until the operator chooses one.</text>
    <breach>review</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="agent-decides-only-what-is-listed">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: the data lake's pack, whose rule this is; the operator, 2026-10-08: "what is left to the agent's determination? not much: we are building the defined product"</from>
    <text>Every task file lists under agent-decides the only things its agent decides: the names and the decomposition inside its own modules and tests, the means of a step within the standard library and the workspace's dependencies, the contents of its fixtures within the cases listed, its commit granularity. Everything else a build needs is written in the task file, the root, the rules, the authority files or the merged code; a build that decides anything else, or decides it differently from the file, is closed, and the step it departed from is named in the review. Every gate names the steps it proves, so a failed gate points at the step to look at.</text>
    <breach>review of the PR against the task file's agent-decides list and its steps; the gates, each naming the steps it proves</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="kickoff">
    <from>The operator, 2026-10-26: a branch and a PR are built from the task's XML file; the operator may name the session that builds a task, the spec's own included</from>
    <text>A build starts with a model pointed at one task file in the repository, specs/recon/tasks/&lt;issue&gt;.xml. Nothing else is said at kickoff. The kickoff line is the first message of a session opened for that build, in a new session, on a checkout of the head of main updated from origin: not the session that wrote the spec, not a session that built or reviewed another build, not one that holds a conversation. There, before the branch is cut, python3 tools/skill_audit.py run on the head of main names what CI's Bright-line skill rules check names on that head, no more and no fewer: it reads no file on a path through a folder named worktrees, and every file elsewhere in the folder, whether git holds it or not; a folder where it names fewer or more is not a build folder, and the build stops there. A model whose session holds anything before the kickoff line beyond what the harness gives every session (its own text, the repository's CLAUDE.md, its settings, a command that set the model or the effort, with its output) stops at that line, says so, and builds nothing. The model writes its own label, &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;, as the root's method model gives it. The operator may instead name a session to build a task, the spec's own included: that session builds when the operator says so, from the task file as written, reads the files in the order the root's method read gives from that word on, and its PR says it was the session the operator named. The file's start element names the branch, the PR title and the first word of every commit; a build started any other way, or on a branch or PR named any other way, does not count.</text>
    <breach>review of the branch name and the PR title against the task file's start element; the PR body stating that the kickoff line was the session's first message, and on which checkout</breach>
  </rule>

  <rule id="every-rule-binds">
    <from>These rules</from>
    <text>Every rule here binds every task. Every gate of a task names in its proves attribute what it proves; a rule that no gate of a task names binds the task all the same.</text>
    <breach>specs/recon/check.py</breach>
  </rule>

</rules>

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!--
  SuperX Recon: the root of the build spec. Version 0.

  An agent reads the merged code its task file names, this root, rules.xml, its one task file under tasks/ and the files
  that task file names, in the order the method's read step gives, and nothing else. The root says where the truth is
  and how the work is done; of the authority files and the merged code it says only what a task relies on, and leaves
  the rest to them.
  Parts: identity, authority, method, sequence, decisions.
  Nothing here writes a count of the captured lines, the sessions, the repositories or the hosts, or a value of the
  operator's instance: a home, a session, a token.
-->
<root product="SuperX Recon" epic="466" version="0" date="2026-10-26">

  <identity>
    <product>The models, the agents, thinking effort and the operator, measured. One crawler crawls every line the kernel captured, every repository those lines lead to and every host those repositories live on, in depth, into one structure, and the module reports on it in its own tab, on its own port. The crawler is the only thing in the module that reads: it finds the details that are not readily apparent, ties what the agents did to what became of their work, and delivers the critical data no one knew to look for.</product>
    <repository path="crates/superx-mod-recon/">The module. schema/, the structure, one file, recon.surql: every table the crawler writes, designed with the operator and approved by the operator change by change, in the place CI guards a module's schema; no task writes it. Made by t01 from the merged superx-mod-performance: Cargo.toml; src/, its code, the executable superx-recon and the crawler, one part per source it crawls; tests/, the tests that cross its parts.</repository>
    <platform path="crates/">The code merged on main is what every task builds on: superx-kernel, which captures every agent's lines into the kernel's database and holds the substrate verbs, the adapters and the module system; superx-ops; superx, the CLI and the daemon; superx-mod-ui, the core UI; superx-mod-performance, which t01 makes this module; and after it what each task merged. An agent reads of it what its task file's reads element names. No task changes superx-kernel, superx or superx-ops; a change outside the module's own crate is named in its task file, or it is not made.</platform>
    <specs path="specs/recon/">rules.xml, the rules; root.xml, this file; tasks/, one XML file per task, named by the task's id and name as the sequence gives them, renamed to its GitHub issue number once the issue exists; build_issues.py, the issues rendered from these files; build_page.py, the Build Spec page rendered from them into build_spec.html; check.py, the spec check; mutate_check.py, its mutation test, which plants one defect of each class the check guards and proves the check names it.</specs>
    <data>What the crawler reads and never writes: the kernel's captured lines, in message and telemetry_stream, with SELECT; the repositories the sessions worked in, with git, read-only; the hosts those repositories live on, through their APIs, with GET. What it writes: its own database, superx/recon, with CREATE only; and its mirror clones, in the repos folder of its module folder, &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/repos/, which nothing else writes. The operator's checkouts are never written, fetched into or run.</data>
    <branches>main, and one branch per build of a task, cut from the head of main and merged into it by the operator; the next task starts from the merged head and reads nothing of an unmerged branch.</branches>
    <issues site="github.com/k8nstantin/superx" epic="466">One issue per task, under the epic; its number names the task file, the branch and the PR.</issues>
  </identity>

  <authority>
    <note>The files that decide. A task reads them by the sections its file names; what they say is the contract. Every quote below is the file's own sentence, verbatim, runs of white space read as one; check.py refuses a quote the file does not hold.</note>

    <file path="BLUEPRINT.md" decides="the architecture: what the kernel does and nothing else, that everything else is a module, the module facility contract, the decisions record, and the module's row in the phase plan">
      <quote>The kernel has zero knowledge of any module's domain.</quote>
      <quote>SELECT + CREATE only, no verb ever issues UPDATE / DELETE / DDL.</quote>
      <quote>A module is a self-contained mini-app: own db `superx/&lt;name&gt;` + service account (via `modules provision`), own dir, own log target, own CLI namespace, own parameters; kernel is the hub — modules depend on the kernel only, never on each other</quote>
      <quote>It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture.</quote>
      <quote>It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path=".claude/skills/zero-trust-execution/SKILL.md" decides="the mandates every change obeys, §1 to §14, which CI and tools/skill_audit.py read by their numbers">
      <quote>NEVER optimize for speed.</quote>
      <quote>Code does not exist until `cargo test --workspace` passes.</quote>
      <quote>Facts go in columns; judgments do not.</quote>
      <quote>A `record&lt;table&gt;` is a `Thing` everywhere.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="SUPERX_SCHEMA.md" decides="the kernel's tables, of which the crawler reads message, telemetry_stream, and the entities and states that name the agents and the sessions">
      <quote>No kernel verb ever emits UPDATE, DELETE, UPSERT, DEFINE, REMOVE, or ALTER.</quote>
      <quote>Every row id is a UUIDv7 generated by the kernel at insert time — never SurrealDB auto-ids.</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" decides="the structure: every table the crawler writes, its fields, its links, its indexes and its vocabularies, approved by the operator change by change; the module applies it as its own schema, byte for byte, and no task writes it">
      <quote>Nothing here is judged.</quote>
      <quote>EVERY ROW IS DERIVED, from the kernel's captured lines and from git,</quote>
      <quote>Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10).</quote>
    </file>

    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs" decides="how Claude Code's lines are captured: a user or assistant line as a message row with the line whole; every other line whole as a transcript_event; a line that is not JSON as a transcript_raw; every file beside the transcripts as a transcript_event of kind file">
      <quote>The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471).</quote>
    </file>
    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/gemini_cli.rs" decides="how Gemini CLI's lines are captured: a user or gemini record as a message row; a header, a header change, an info, error or warning line, and every earlier write of a record, whole, as a transcript_event; an unknown shape as a transcript_raw"/>
    <file path="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_desktop.rs" decides="how Claude Desktop's log records are captured: each record whole as a desktop_event"/>

    <params decides="every tunable of the module: attr_recon_* on its registry entity, set with superx param set recon &lt;name&gt; &lt;json&gt;, each running on its fallback until it is set; a limit's fallback is no limit">The module's params.rs names each one and its fallback; a task that adds one names it, its fallback and what it tunes in its file.</params>
    <credentials decides="the hosts' credentials: &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, written by the operator, mode 600, read by the crawler alone">Its shape is decision credentials-file.</credentials>

    <code path="crates/" decides="how the module runs: what the merged code does is what is merged, never what a spec says about it">Read as the task file's reads element names.</code>

    <not_sources>Earlier spec versions, earlier build branches, the issues' own text, conversations, memory files, the pages, CLAUDE.md, the README files, docs/, any document outside the repository.</not_sources>
  </authority>

  <method>
    <note>How a task is done. Every step binds every task; a task file never loosens one.</note>

    <read>A build starts at the task file (rules.xml kickoff); its start element sends the model here. Then, in this order: the merged code its task file's reads element names, this root, rules.xml and your task file, each whole; then the files your task file names, by the sections it lists. Nothing else: nothing authority not_sources names. The order is required: a session that reads a file out of this order reads again from the first file in order before it writes anything.</read>

    <silence>Everything the task needs is in those files; an agent assumes nothing and figures nothing out. When something is missing or two of them disagree, stop: build nothing around it, and hand the operator the branch and the PR with exactly which element is silent or which two lines conflict and what was needed (method pr). An agent never changes the structure, the kernel or an authority file; a task that needs one changed is a stop, and the operator holds a design session. What an agent decides on its own is only what its task file lists under agent-decides.</silence>

    <agent>A session opened for the build, with the kickoff line as its first message and nothing before it but what rules.xml kickoff allows the harness: not the spec's own session, not one that built or reviewed another build, unless the operator names that session to build the task (rules.xml kickoff). One session, one agent, sequential: the agent that holds the task does every step itself, reading, writing, testing, running the gates, writing the PR. It never spawns, delegates to, forks or messages another agent, subagent, worker, team or background session, and never asks another model to build or verify a part. A PR whose history shows another agent's hand is closed.</agent>

    <build>Each version of a task file is built once: one agent, one branch from the head of main, one PR into main, reviewed and merged by the operator before the next task starts. The next task's agent starts from the merged head. The model is the operator's choice per task and the label records it. Exception, only when the task file's comparison attribute is yes: one agent per model, each from the same file version on its own branch with its own PR, none merged until the operator compares the results and chooses one; the others are closed unmerged.</build>

    <branch>feat/&lt;issue&gt;-&lt;name&gt;-v&lt;file version&gt;-&lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;, cut from the head of main: the task's issue number, the task's name as its file gives it with spaces as hyphens, the task file's version attribute, the label the model wrote at kickoff. The task file's start element writes it out. One branch per build; never amended after its PR is merged; a build of a later file version has a branch of its own beside it.</branch>

    <model>The model label is &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;: the model's own name and the effort it runs at, as its session reports them, lowercase, dots and spaces as hyphens; the model writes it itself at kickoff; when its session does not report its effort, it asks the operator for that before it cuts the branch, and is told nothing else. The label names the branch and the PR title's suffix. The agent never changes it during a build.</model>

    <commits>Every commit message starts with #&lt;issue&gt;. No attribution trailer of any kind. Small commits, each a step that leaves the local gates green. Paths are staged by name, never all at once.</commits>

    <tests>Rust tests, run with cargo test, written as the module's merged tests are: a module's own rules in its tests module, what crosses its parts under crates/superx-mod-recon/tests/, on SurrealDB's in-memory engine with the kernel's schema and the module's own applied, as the merged tests apply them. A task file's tests element lists every rule its steps state, one item each, and the agent writes one test per item, named by it, and none outside the list: a rule the list lacks is a stop, not a test. A fixture property one item needs is stated in that item; a property every test shares is stated once in the tests element's how. The fixtures are the test's own: lines built in the shapes the adapters write, repositories made with git in a scratch folder, hosts answered by a server of the test's own on the loopback; no test reads the operator's instance, transcripts, repositories or a real host, and no fixture copies a real line, name or token.</tests>

    <lint>cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings exits 0, and python3 tools/skill_audit.py names no breach in a file the task owns, before every commit. The audit names each breach under the mandate it breaks, by its number; main's own breaches, in files no task owns, stand as main's.</lint>

    <gates>Run the task's gates in their order; each is a command and an expectation that is a relation over what the command prints, never a number written down.
      A gate of kind local runs in the checkout, from the repository root; its output goes into the gate table (method pr).
      A gate of kind live runs on the operator's instance after the operator has deployed the build it names (method deploy), at the agent's request and on the operator's word: the agent runs the command the gate gives, superx-recon at the instance's home, and reads only what superx-recon prints and what its own URL answers; it never reads the kernel's database or the module's with any other client, and never runs a command the gate does not give. A gate run any other way does not count. A gate that fails is a stop.</gates>

    <ownership>Create, modify and touch only the paths the task file lists under owns; a path it lists under forbid is never opened for writing; a path it does not list is not touched. A PR that touches another path is closed.</ownership>

    <pr>One PR per branch, into main, titled "#&lt;issue&gt; &lt;name&gt; v&lt;file version&gt; · &lt;model&gt;-&lt;effort&gt;" as the task file's start element writes it, opened by the agent with gh once every local gate is green, or when the build stops (method silence, gates). Its body, in this order: the spec version built, the root's version and the task file's version attribute; the model label; the session: that the kickoff line was its first message, the order the files were read (method read) and the main head the branch was cut from; the gate table: id, the command as run, exit, the matched line or the gate's output, local or live, and for a live gate the build it ran on; what stopped, if anything: the element that is silent or the two lines that conflict, and what was needed; the lint and test commands as run with their last line; for every step of the task file, the function or test that carries it, so a reviewer who finds a fault knows which step to look at; and the Skill compliance section CI requires, each of the three gates with its result. The operator reviews and merges; the agent never merges.</pr>

    <deliverable>A task delivers code, tests and the PR with its gate table. Nothing else: no change to the structure beyond its task file, none to the kernel, no data, no deploy.</deliverable>

    <rerun>A task is re-run when its task file changes: a new file version, and a new branch from the head of main carrying that version in its name, so it stands beside the earlier build's branch; the earlier branch is not amended, and one that is not merged is closed unmerged. A change to the root or the rules alone re-runs a built task only when the build as made no longer follows them as changed; the review names such a task, and its task file takes a new version. Nothing is patched on a merged branch. A task already merged is re-run only after the operator has reverted, on main, its merge and the merge of every task after it, so that the re-run starts from what its file's before element states; the tasks after it are then re-run in the order of the sequence.</rerun>

    <deploy>
      <note>The operator runs the module from a build of one commit. At the agent's request (method gates) the operator deploys it by the steps below in their order; an agent never builds the release, installs, provisions, starts or stops anything on the instance, and never touches the superx daemon or its binary. &lt;home&gt; is the instance's home and &lt;bin&gt; the folder its executables are installed in. The agent's request names these commands with every value filled.</note>
      <step n="1" name="build">
        <where>a worktree checked out at the commit, never a checkout on another branch</where>
        <cmd>cargo build --release -p superx-mod-recon</cmd>
        <result>target/release/superx-recon, built from that commit</result>
      </step>
      <step n="2" name="install">
        <cmd>cp -p &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon &lt;home&gt;/backup/superx-recon-&lt;the running build's commit&gt;</cmd>
        <cmd>cp target/release/superx-recon &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon.new &amp;&amp; mv -f &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon.new &lt;bin&gt;/superx-recon</cmd>
      </step>
      <step n="3" name="provision">
        <note>Only when the task file says so: when the structure changed, or when the build derives what the rows already written lack, since no row is ever changed. The database is rebuilt from the file, run by the operator in a terminal, the root password typed there; every row is crawled again (rules derived-and-rebuilt).</note>
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; --initialize</cmd>
      </step>
      <step n="4" name="restart">
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; stop</cmd>
        <cmd>superx-recon --home &lt;home&gt; start</cmd>
      </step>
    </deploy>
  </method>

  <sequence>
    <note>Four tasks, four PRs at most: the structure first, whole, before anything fills it; then the crawler that fills it, the lines in one task and the repositories with their hosts in the next; then the tab, designed with the operator once the crawler is merged. Each task is one build an agent does from its file; the operator deploys and runs what it builds. A task starts when the task before it is merged on main. Its file under tasks/ carries, verbatim, what the structure and the authority files say about its piece, what it builds, its gates and what it owns; this list carries the piece, the files it reflects and what it builds.</note>

    <task id="t01" issue="536" name="structure" file="specs/recon/tasks/536.xml" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql BLUEPRINT.md" command="superx-recon --initialize; superx-recon start; superx-recon stop; superx-recon status; superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/">
      <piece>The module becomes superx-mod-recon, and its structure is built whole before anything fills it: the crate, from superx-mod-performance, its code moved and renamed and its crawler doing what it does today; the executable superx-recon; the database superx/recon, from the structure, crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql, which the crate applies byte for byte and never writes: the deep crawl's tables as approved, the hosts' and the mirrors' tables, every answer of every read API kept whole beside typed tables for what is measured, and git's facts; the parameters attr_recon_*; the module folder with its repos folder and its credentials file; the status, listing every table and which hosts have credentials. superx-mod-performance and superx-mod-repos leave the workspace, and the core UI links to no module's own UI (#534).</piece>
      <quote from="BLUEPRINT.md">It is never linked into `superx`, so nothing it does can reach capture.</quote>
      <quote from="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Nothing is overwritten and nothing is removed (§10).</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t02" issue="537" name="lines" file="specs/recon/tasks/537.xml" depends="t01" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/">
      <piece>The crawler crawls the captured lines in depth. Every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, is read once into the tables it feeds, and is itself a line row that names those tables, none when it fed none; every field seen on a kind of line is a line_field row that says whether a column holds it; every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same range, and a difference makes the pass partial and is named. It fills the lines' tables whole: sessions and their places, prompts, triggers, replies and what they said, their states, thinking, tool calls and their results, edits, git events, tests and builds, turns, compactions, recaps, requests with their pieces and shapes, context, hooks, session states, costs by session and by model, links, git states, notices, questions, task changes, plans, subagent runs and queue changes.</piece>
      <quote from="crates/superx-kernel/src/adapters/claude_code.rs">The whole line, whatever its length: nothing is dropped (#471).</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t03" issue="538" name="repositories" file="specs/recon/tasks/538.xml" depends="t02" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/repos/ crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/hosts/">
      <piece>The crawler crawls the repositories and their hosts. From the working directories the lines name, and the roots the operator names, every checkout and repository; one worker per repository, as many at once as attr_recon_repos_at_once allows. For each repository its mirror clone in the repos folder, fetched with the credentials, and every fact git holds: commits with their files, renames and copies, their time zones, patch ids, signatures and trailers; how each merge's conflicts were resolved; annotated tags; refs with their upstreams and how far each is ahead and behind, and every reflog entry; HEAD moves and operations in progress; where each commit sits and what landed through another commit, what each commit still holds at the main line's tip, the files at the tip, uncommitted work, remotes and set-up, and every git call. From the hosts, with the credentials and GET only, what the hosts' tables hold. Every commit tied to the session that made it.</piece>
      <quote from="BLUEPRINT.md">It correlates what the agents did with what the code bases show, diligence before speed.</quote>
    </task>

    <task id="t04" issue="539" name="tab" file="specs/recon/tasks/539.xml" depends="t03" reflects="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql" command="superx-recon run" builds="crates/superx-mod-recon/">
      <piece>The tab: what the module shows on its own port, from what t02 and t03 filled. Designed with the operator once t03 is merged (decision the-tab).</piece>

  <decisions>
    <note>What was decided, when, and where it lives; what is still open. A confirmed decision is written into the file or element named, which is the authority; it is listed here so an agent knows a question is closed. An open decision is a question an agent never answers: a task that needs it stops. An open decision names in closes-before the task before whose file it is closed, and check.py refuses that file while the decision is open.</note>

    <decision id="recon" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="identity; sequence t01">The module is superx-mod-recon: the executable superx-recon, the database superx/recon, the parameters attr_recon_*, the module folder &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/. It replaces superx-mod-performance, whose name the operator rejected.</decision>
    <decision id="one-module-one-crawler" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="BLUEPRINT.md; rules one-module-its-own-process">One module, one schema, one crawler: the lines and the repositories are crawled by one crawler into one database, through its sources, and a new source plugs in beside them.</decision>
    <decision id="own-process" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-24" where="rules one-module-its-own-process">Its own executable, process, database, port and tab, never linked into superx; it reads the kernel's data and the kernel never depends on it.</decision>
    <decision id="crawler-is-the-reader" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules crawler-is-the-reader">The crawler is the only thing that reads; nothing beside it, and no part of it, is called a reader.</decision>
    <decision id="crawl-not-skim" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules crawl-not-skim">Slow, diligent, methodical: every line, field, commit and ref accounted for and checked against its source; the details that are not readily apparent found and tied together.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-from-the-logs" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules repos-from-the-logs">The repositories come from the session logs; the logs report a change first and the crawler follows them.</decision>
    <decision id="hosts-and-mirrors" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials, never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t01, t03">The credentials serve both: the hosts' APIs, read-only, and the crawler's own mirror clones, fetched with them. The approved structure's sentence that git is read with never a fetch holds for the operator's checkouts; t01 rewrites it to say the mirrors are fetched and nothing else is.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-folder" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="identity data">The mirror clones live in a folder of their own, under the module's folder, which nothing else writes.</decision>
    <decision id="mirrors-take-everything" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03">The crawler fetches into its own mirrors freely: one mirror per remote, a fetch takes every ref the remote advertises, and nothing a mirror has fetched is ever pruned or collected, so a branch rewritten or deleted on the host keeps what the crawler saw. The more it holds, the more it can find.</decision>
    <decision id="parallel-by-repository" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules parallel-by-repository; sequence t03">The crawler scales by repository: one worker per repository, as many at once as a parameter allows.</decision>
    <decision id="structure-first" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence">The structure first, whole; then the crawler that fills it; then the tab, designed after.</decision>
    <decision id="four-prs" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence">Four PRs at most; fewer PRs, more work in each.</decision>
    <decision id="specs-are-the-truth" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules specs-are-the-truth; identity specs">The XML files are the source of truth, in the data lake's pack: a root, the rules, one file per task, and the tools that check and render them; a branch and a PR are built from a task file.</decision>
    <decision id="deep-structure" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">The deep crawl's tables, approved whole: every field the agents write and everything git holds gets a column, a table, or a stated reason to stay in the kernel line.</decision>
    <decision id="old-data" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-25" where="rules derived-and-rebuilt">The kernel's lines captured before a capture fix are read as they are: nothing is recaptured or repaired.</decision>

    <decision id="hosts-take-everything" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t01, t03; rules crawl-not-skim">From the hosts, everything their read APIs return for each repository the crawler crawls: every answer kept whole, beside typed tables for what is measured, pull requests, their commits, reviews, comments, checks and pipelines, merges, issues, releases and the rest the answers hold, and every call recorded. Less data is more assumption; the operator has intuition already, and the crawler exists for the facts the operator does not know. The tables themselves are designed into the structure from the hosts' own API documentation, and the operator approves the structure (§7, §11).</decision>
    <decision id="credentials-file" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="authority credentials; rules hosts-read-only-with-credentials">&lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, in JSON as the instance's own files are, written by the operator: one entry per host, each a token with every read scope the host offers and no write scope; the crawler refuses the file when anyone but its owner can read it. A mirror's fetch gets its credential from superx-recon itself, answering git as its askpass program, so a token is never written into git's configuration, a URL, a command line or the environment.</decision>
    <decision id="live-gates" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="method gates, deploy">A task's live gates run before its merge, on the operator's instance, once the operator has deployed the branch's build of superx-recon; superx and its daemon are never touched, and every row the build writes is derived, so the next deploy rebuilds what it needs.</decision>
    <decision id="retire-performance" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t01">At t01's deploy superx-performance is stopped and superx-recon started in its place; once recon's first pass is complete, the operator drops superx/performance and superx/repos under root. Every row they hold is derived, and recon derives it again from the same lines and repositories.</decision>
    <decision id="repos-at-once" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules parallel-by-repository, nothing-dropped">attr_recon_repos_at_once falls back to no limit: every repository a pass crawls gets its own worker at once, unless the operator sets a number. A limit's fallback is no limit.</decision>
    <decision id="structure" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">The structure as designed on 2026-10-26: the deep crawl's tables as approved, the changes to tables 26, 28, 30, 31, 33, 55 and 59, and the hosts' and mirrors' tables 60–82, read from the hosts' own API documentation and checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4, a good row of every table taken and every bad row refused by the rule it breaks. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26 (§7, §11). It lives where CI guards a module's schema, so every change to it carries the operator's approval marker.</decision>
    <decision id="git-facts" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Git's own documentation read whole (git 2.50.1), and what git holds that the structure lacked added to it: where each commit was made in local time, files copied beside files moved, how each merge's conflicts were resolved, the commits that make the same change, operations a checkout is in the middle of, each branch's upstream and how far it is ahead and behind, line counts blind to whitespace, annotated tags and signatures, commit trailers; tables 83–85 and changes to tables 18, 21, 22, 28, 30, 31 and 56, checked on an empty SurrealDB 3.1.4. Approved by the operator on 2026-10-26: "1 -9 yes change the schema for more data".</decision>
    <decision id="git-cli" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="rules never-write-a-measured-repository; sequence t03">The crawler reads git through git itself, one process per call, never a library: every call is a git_call row, and git's answers are git's own. The operator, 2026-10-26: "Recommendation: keep the CLI. yes".</decision>
    <decision id="http-client" status="confirmed" date="2026-10-26" where="sequence t03">The hosts' APIs are called through reqwest, with rustls, the one HTTP client for both hosts: no host's own client library, since every answer is kept whole with its headers, and no maintained Bitbucket Cloud client exists. Its redirects drop the credential when they leave the host. Recommended with the git CLI and answered with the same yes.</decision>
    <decision id="devlake-ideas" status="open" closes-before="t03" where="crates/superx-mod-recon/schema/recon.surql">Four more facts from Apache DevLake's design, proposed on the structure page on 2026-10-26 as DDL, not yet in the structure: labels and milestones, issue events, the e-mails an account commits as, the commits each deployment shipped.</decision>
    <decision id="the-tab" status="open" closes-before="t04" where="sequence t04">What the tab shows and how, designed with the operator from what t02 and t03 filled.</decision>
  </decisions>
</root>

      <step n="5" name="the module folder">At run, after the module is registered as starting and before it says it is active: the module folder holds repos/, made with mode 700 when it is absent and left as it is, mode included, when it is present; and credentials.json, written when it is absent, with mode 600, holding the template, and never written when it is present, whatever it holds. The template is this JSON object, written by serde_json::to_string_pretty with a newline after it: "_doc" with the text "Read-only tokens, one per host. github.com: a fine-grained personal access token with read access to metadata, contents, pull requests, issues, actions, commit statuses, deployments and administration. bitbucket.org: an API token with the read scopes. Keep this file mode 600. superx-recon reads it, never writes it once it exists, and never shows a token."; "github.com" with the object holding "token": ""; "bitbucket.org" with the object holding "token": "". Instance gains the two paths, &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/repos and &lt;home&gt;/modules/recon/credentials.json, beside the pid file and the logs.</step>
      <step n="6" name="the credentials">A module of the crate reads credentials.json, when status renders and when a part of the crawler asks for a host's token (t03). Its answer is one of: none, the file is absent; refused, the file's mode lets anyone but its owner read or write it (its mode and 0o077 is not 0), and nothing of it is read; unreadable, the file is not a JSON object whose every key that does not start with "_" names an object with a string "token", with serde_json's reason or the key that broke it; or the hosts, each github.com or bitbucket.org, with its token or no token when the string is empty, and every other key that does not start with "_" named as an unknown host. A key that starts with "_" is a note and is skipped. A token is held in a type whose Debug and Display print &lt;token&gt; and nothing of the token, and no log line, status text, error or test output ever holds one.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the status">status lists every table of the structure but pass and pass_source, each once, in groups, each table with the name its rows are called: sessions, context, models and tools as today; repos as today, and after its tables merge_resolution "merge resolutions", tag "tags" and commit_trailer "trailers"; then hosts: host_repository "repositories on hosts", host_repository_state "host settings", host_user "host accounts", host_crawl "host crawls", host_call "host calls", host_object "host objects", mirror "mirrors", pull_request "pull requests", pull_request_state "pull request states", pull_request_commit "pull request commits", pull_request_file "pull request files", pull_request_review "reviews", pull_request_event "pull request events", issue "issues", issue_state "issue states", host_comment "comments", ci_run "CI runs", ci_run_state "CI run states", ci_log "CI logs", release "releases", host_rule "host rules", deployment "deployments", deployment_state "deployment states"; then census as today. After the groups and before the last pass, two lines: `credentials &lt;path&gt;: ` and then, when the answer of step 6 is the hosts, github.com then bitbucket.org, each `&lt;host&gt; token set` or `&lt;host&gt; no token`, then each unknown host as `&lt;host&gt; unknown`, joined by ` · `; when it is none, `none`; refused, `refused, mode &lt;its mode in octal&gt;; chmod 600 &lt;path&gt;`; unreadable, `unreadable: &lt;the reason&gt;`. Then `repos       &lt;path&gt;`, the repos folder.</step>
      <step n="8" name="the words">No file of the crate calls anything a `reader`: the doc comments that do, in crawler/value.rs, crawler/lines/mod.rs, crawler/lines/facts.rs, crawler/lines/kernel.rs, crawler/lines/state.rs, crawler/lines/formats/mod.rs and crawler/lines/formats/claude_code.rs, say the crawler or what it does, their meaning unchanged.</step>

  <piece>The lines task. The crawler crawls every line the kernel holds, of every agent and every kind, side files and lines that are not JSON included, in depth. Each line is a line row naming the tables it fed. Each field a kind of line carries is a line_field row saying whether a column holds it. Every table the structure gives the lines is filled from the shapes the lines are on record in, and the tables the first crawler fills gain the columns the structure added to them. A reply read again steps over the lines between its own. Every pass checks the lines it read against the lines the kernel holds in the same ids. Nothing of the repositories, their mirrors or their hosts is crawled here: that is t03.</piece>

  <before>Merged on main when this task starts: t01 (#536), the module superx-mod-recon on the approved structure, on the operator's instance superx-recon running on superx/recon. Its crawler does what superx-performance's did. It reads the kernel's message rows and, of its telemetry, Claude Code's system lines turn_duration, compact_boundary and away_summary and its queued_command attachments. It writes session; session_place; prompt, all but prompt_id, source, permission_mode, turn_index and prompt_index; reply, all but request, prompt_id, client_version, skill, plugin, mcp_server, mcp_tool, refusal, refusal_text, cache_miss and cache_missed; reply_state, all but cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens; thinking; tool_call, all but skill, model and timeout_ms; tool_result, all but decision, decided_by, decision_source, decision_reason, denial, operator_feedback, sandboxed, persisted_bytes, return_note, background_task and text; edit, from the file tools alone; git_event, test_run, build_run, turn, compaction and away. And it lists the repositories. Every other table of the lines is empty.</before>

  <after>Who uses what this task builds: t03 (#538), which follows the sessions' places, git events, git states and links to the repositories and their hosts, and ties commits to sessions; t04 (#539), the tab, which reads every table this task fills.</after>

  <build>
    <module path="crates/superx-mod-recon/src/crawler/lines/" name="the lines part of the crawler">
      <step n="1" name="every line">Each pass reads, in the order of their ids as today and in pages as bounded as today's, every message row, its raw_text beside its raw, and every telemetry_stream row whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event, its payload whole, a side file's content included; every other telemetry row is no line and is skipped, as today. A line is read from its raw_text, parsed as JSON, wherever the kernel kept one, and from its parsed copy, a message row's raw or a payload's line, only where the kernel kept no raw_text: the kernel kept the parsed copy alone before #529, both from #529, the raw_text alone while #542 was its build, and both again from #546, and a line kept in any of these shapes goes through the same steps below. A side file and a Claude Desktop record are read from their payload's line, as today. A Gemini transcript_event of kind superseded holds an earlier write of a user or gemini record, whole, and is read as that record is read, in its place among the lines. Each agent's format knows the kinds of line the steps below read, and these, which feed no table: Claude Code's file-history-snapshot, file-history-delta, last-prompt, atis-latch, agent-name, fork-context-ref, started, launched, result, failed, artifact-autoreact-ledger and artifact-comment-monitor lines and its side files; Gemini's header and $set lines; every Claude Desktop record. A line of a kind no format knows, a line that is not JSON, and a line in which a format finds a shape it does not read, as today, is counted unread.</step>
      <step n="2" name="the line">Every line read is one line row, written once ever: when the line is first read, as that read found it, and after every other row that read wrote from it. For each page it reads, the crawler asks its database which of the page's kernel ids line already holds; a line read again writes no line row, though it may write a fact its first read could not, such as one of a session the kernel has named since. kernel_message: its id. at: as today, the line's own time where it has one, else when the kernel captured it. agent: the agent's name the kernel gives it; a line the kernel names no agent for is no line row, and is counted unread. session: the session row of the session the kernel names for it, NONE where it names none, as for a Claude Desktop record or a project's own side file. kind: the line's type; for a Claude Code system line its type, a slash and its subtype, and for an attachment its type, a slash and its attachment's type; where the line holds no type, the kind the kernel gave it (a Gemini header or $set, a line kept by its kind alone before #471); a Claude Code line that is not JSON, transcript_raw; a Claude Desktop record, desktop_event. agent_uuid: its uuid. parent_uuid: its parentUuid, else its logicalParentUuid. prompt_id: its promptId. sidechain: its isSidechain, false where it has none. client_version: its version. bytes: the length in bytes of the line's raw_text, where the kernel kept one; for a side file, the bytes its line gives; for a Claude Desktop record, the length in bytes of its text; for a line kept by its kind alone, 0; for a line captured before the kernel kept raw_text, the length of the line as captured written out as compact JSON (value::json_len). read_as: the tables this read wrote a row to from the line, each once, in the order the structure's read_as list gives them; empty when it wrote none.</step>
      <step n="3" name="the fields">Every field of every line that is JSON is counted by its path. Each (agent, kind, path) is one line_field row the first time a line shows it, first_at the line's at, first_line its kernel id, stored whether a column holds it; and one row more each time stored changes for it. The crawler holds the newest row of each, read from its database at its first pass, and writes one only when it differs. A path is the keys from the line's top to the field, joined by a dot. A list's items take the list's path with [] after it. A block of a message's content list is named by its type in brackets, message.content[text]; a tool call by its name, message.content[tool_use:Bash]; a tool result by the name of the call it answers, message.content[tool_result:Bash], with ? where the crawler holds no such call; a line's toolUseResult the same way, toolUseResult[Bash]. A key is written &lt;k&gt; when it is data: a key that is not a name, a name being made of ASCII letters, digits, _, $ and - and beginning with a letter, _ or $; a key that is a UUID; and every key of a map the agent's format names as keyed by data, for Claude Code modelUsage, snapshot.trackedFileBackups, artifacts, wireToolInputs and wireIngestContext, the answers and the annotations of an AskUserQuestion result, and the files of an Artifact call's input. Each agent's format declares, per kind, the paths whose values its reading puts in a column, a path whose value a column holds whole standing for itself and every path under it; those paths are stored, and every other is not.</step>
      <step n="4" name="the operator's prompts">A prompt is read as today, and takes the columns the structure added: prompt_id its promptId, source its promptSource, permission_mode its permissionMode, turn_index and prompt_index its turnPosition.turnIndex and turnPosition.promptIndex, each NONE where the line has none.</step>
      <step n="5" name="the triggers">A line that is no prompt as today's prompts are read is a trigger row when the first of these holds. A user line that is no tool result and whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind. A queued_command attachment whose origin.kind is set and is not human: kind its origin.kind; one with no origin whose commandMode is set and is not prompt: kind its commandMode. A user line marked isCompactSummary: kind compact_summary. A user line that is no tool result, marked isSidechain, with no parentUuid and no origin, the instruction an agent gave its subagent: kind subagent. source: its origin.from, else its origin.producer, else its origin.name, else NONE. prompt_id: its promptId. text: whole, a user line's string content or its text blocks joined by a newline, a queued command's prompt or its text parts joined by a newline. chars: the characters of text. Any other user line that is no tool result and no prompt feeds no table, unless step 16 makes it a notice.</step>
      <step n="6" name="what the models said">A said row per text block of a Claude Code assistant line: position the block's place in its reply, placed as a thinking span is placed today; text whole; chars its characters. A Gemini reply is a said row, position 0, each time a write of it holds a content longer than its newest said row, superseded writes included.</step>
      <step n="7" name="the reply">A reply takes the columns the structure added, from the line it is written from: request, the request row whose request_key is the line's requestRef, NONE where the line has none or the crawler holds no such request; prompt_id, the newest promptId of the session's lines read before it, a session first read since the crawler began taking its newest from its line rows; client_version its version; skill, plugin, mcp_server and mcp_tool its attributionSkill, attributionPlugin, attributionMcpServer and attributionMcpTool; refusal and refusal_text its message.stop_details.category and message.stop_details.explanation where message.stop_details.type is refusal; cache_miss and cache_missed its message.diagnostics.cache_miss_reason.type and cache_missed_input_tokens. A reply_state takes cache_write_1h_tokens and cache_write_5m_tokens from its usage's cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens and ephemeral_5m_input_tokens. A Claude Code line whose model is &lt;synthetic&gt; stays no reply, as today (step 16).</step>
      <step n="8" name="the reply read again">A reply read again from its middle (state.rs hold) reads the session's lines from the reply's first line to the line being read, that one included, keeps those of the reply and steps over every other, a subagent's lines among them; it no longer stops at the first line that is not the reply's.</step>
      <step n="9" name="calls and results">A tool call takes skill (a Skill call's input.skill, a Gemini activate_skill call's args.name), model (an Agent call's input.model) and timeout_ms (its input.timeout). A result takes, from its line, when the line carries that result alone, as today's sandbox hosts are read: decision, decided_by, decision_source and decision_reason from permissionDecision.decision, reasonType, source and reasonCode; denial from toolDenialKind; operator_feedback from userFeedback; sandboxed from sandboxed. From its toolUseResult, persisted_bytes, return_note and background_task: its persistedOutputSize, returnCodeInterpretation and backgroundTaskId. text: what the tool returned, as text as today's results read it, when its outcome is not ok. A shell command's result that ended ok, whose toolUseResult carries a bashEditDiff not marked shared, is an edit row per entry of its files: path its filePath; kind created where it is marked created, deleted where it is marked deleted, else modified; added and removed the lines of its hunks that begin with + and with -; counted_from shell; operator_changed false. A diff marked shared saw files change while other commands ran too, so the call alone did not change them, and it is no edit row.</step>
      <step n="10" name="the requests">An api-request line is a request row: request_key its id; model, effort, max_tokens, thinking and thinking_display its params.model, params.output_config.effort, params.max_tokens, params.thinking.type and params.thinking.display; betas its params.betas; context_edits the type of each of its params.context_management.edits; query_source its querySource; cache_breakpoints the number of its cacheControl entries; keep and tail its keep and tail; shape_hash its shapeHash; digest its requestDigest; params its params whole. An api-request-blob line is a request_part row, one per hash ever: role its message.role, NONE where it has none; blocks the type of each block of its message.content, text where the content is a string; chars the characters of the text it carries: a string content, a text block's text, a thinking block's thinking, a tool_addition block's tool.definition.description. An api-request-shape line is a request_shape row, one per shapeHash ever: system_parts the number of its shape.system entries, system_chars the characters of their text, tools the name of each of its shape.tools.</step>
      <step n="11" name="the context">Every attachment line but a queued_command is a context row: kind its attachment's type; names, removed and failed empty, changed and reason and tokens_left NONE, chars 0, but as follows. instructions: names the path of each of its files, chars their content, changed and reason its own. skill_listing: names its names, chars its content. invoked_skills: names the name of each of its skills, chars their content. deferred_tools_delta: names its addedNames, readdedNames and surfacedNames, removed its removedNames and the name of each of its retractedTools, failed the name of each of its failedMcpServers, chars its addedLines and the description of each of its surfacedDefinitions' definition. deferred_tools_record: names the name of each of its entries, chars their description. mcp_instructions_delta: names its addedNames, removed its removedNames, chars its addedBlocks. agent_listing_delta: names its addedTypes, removed its removedTypes, chars its addedLines. file: names its filename, chars its content.file.content. edited_text_file: names its filename, chars its snippet. compact_file_reference: names its filename. environment: names the field of each of its changes. date: names its date, changed its own. model: names its identity.modelId, chars its text. prompt_snapshot: names the name of each of its tools, chars each of its systemPrompt entries and each tool's description. hook_additional_context and hook_success: names its hookName, chars its content or its stdout. plan_file_reference: names its planFilePath, chars its planContent. plan_mode and plan_mode_exit: names its planFilePath. task_status: names its taskId, chars its description. command_permissions: names its allowedTools. remote_session_change: names its pr and its commit, each where set. session_context: chars its context.gitStatus and context.userEmail. total_tokens_reminder: tokens_left the number its text gives before " tokens left", chars its text. Every other type: chars its text, content or banner, where it has one that is a string. chars counts characters.</step>
      <step n="12" name="the hooks">A system/stop_hook_summary line is a hook_run row per entry of its hookInfos, position the entry's place: event Stop, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors the number of the line's hookErrors, prevented the line's preventedContinuation, chars 0. A hook_additional_context attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command and ms NONE, errors 0, prevented false, chars its content. A hook_success attachment is one, position 0: event its hookEvent, command its command, ms its durationMs, errors 0, prevented false, chars its stdout.</step>
      <step n="13" name="the session">An ai-title, permission-mode or mode line is a session_state row when it changes the session's newest title (its aiTitle), permission mode (its permissionMode) or mode (its mode): the row holds the three as they stand after it. A cost-state line is a session_cost row when its figures differ from the session's newest: usd its totalCostUSD; api_ms and api_ms_no_retries its totalAPIDuration and totalAPIDurationWithoutRetries; tool_ms its totalToolDuration; wall_ms its totalDuration; lines_added and lines_removed its totalLinesAdded and totalLinesRemoved; unknown_cost its hasUnknownModelCost. With it, a model_cost row per key of its modelUsage whose entry holds the figures: model the key; usd, input, output, cache_read, cache_write, thinking and web_searches the entry's costUSD, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadInputTokens, cacheCreationInputTokens, thinkingTokens and webSearchRequests. An entry that holds none of them is no row; one that holds some and not others leaves the line unread.</step>
      <step n="14" name="the links">A pr-link line is a session_link of kind pr: url its prUrl, number its prNumber, repository its prRepository. A frame-link line that holds a frameUrl is one of kind artifact: url its frameUrl, title its title. A result of a call to a tool whose name ends createJiraIssue, editJiraIssue or transitionJiraIssue is one of kind issue per entry of its line's mcpMeta.structuredContent.issues.nodes: url the entry's self, key its key, title its fields.summary. One row per session, kind and url ever, first_at and kernel_message the first line that names it.</step>
      <step n="15" name="the git state">A result line's serverClassifierContext.context.git_state that carries no error is a git_state row when it differs from the session's newest: cwd, root, branch and default_branch its own; clean its status.clean; modified and staged its status.counts.modified and status.counts.staged; untracked its status.counts.untracked_normal; remote_host, remote and visibility its visibility.origin.host, visibility.origin.remote and visibility.origin.visibility. One that carries an error holds no state yet, and is no row.</step>
      <step n="16" name="the notices">A notice row for: a Claude Code assistant line marked isApiErrorMessage, kind its error, status its apiErrorStatus, text the text of its content, limit its quotaLimits.rateLimitType, resets_at its quotaLimits.resetsAt, a count of seconds since the Unix epoch, as a time; a system/model_refusal_no_fallback line, kind model_refusal_no_fallback, model its originalModel, text its apiRefusalExplanation; a system/informational line, kind informational, text its content; a system/local_command line, and a user line whose content is a string that begins &lt;local-command-stdout&gt;, kind local_command, text its content; a Gemini info, error or warning line, kind its type, text its content. Every column a line does not give is NONE.</step>
      <step n="17" name="the agent's own tools">Each of these is read when the call's result is: at the call's at. An AskUserQuestion call is a question row per entry of its input's questions, position the entry's place: header, question, multi its multiSelect, options the label of each of its options, answer what its result's answers give for that question, note the notes its result's annotations give for it, each NONE where the result gives none. A Gemini ask_user call is the same per entry of its args.questions, multi its multiSelect, false where it has none, answer what its result's output gives under the question's place in its answers, note NONE. An ExitPlanMode call is a plan row: text its input's plan, chars its characters, file its input's planFilePath, edited whether its result is an object whose plan differs from its input's. A TaskCreate call whose result ended ok is a task_event row, action created, task_key its result's task.id, subject its input's subject, status NONE, text its input's description; a TaskUpdate call whose result ended ok is one with action updated, task_key its input's taskId, subject, status and text its input's subject, status and description; a Gemini update_topic call that ended ok is one with action topic, task_key and status NONE, subject its args.title, text its args.summary and args.strategic_intent joined by a newline. An Agent call whose toolUseResult is an object is a subagent_run row: agent_key its agentId, agent_type its agentType else its call's input.subagent_type, requested_model its call's input.model, model its resolvedModel, status its status, background its isAsync, false where it has none, ms its totalDurationMs, tokens its totalTokens, tool_calls its totalToolUseCount, tool_stats its toolStats, usage its usage, each NONE where it has none. A queue-operation line is a queue_event row: operation and reason its own, chars the characters of its content, 0 where it has none.</step>
      <step n="18" name="the check">For every page it reads, the pass counts with count() the rows the kernel holds over the same ids, the page's first to its last: message rows on a page of them, and on a page of the telemetry stream the rows whose lifecycle_event is transcript_event, transcript_raw or desktop_event. Their sum is the source's lines_held; items stays the lines it read. When they differ the source's outcome is partial, its note naming both. pass_source.counts gains lines_held; lines_unused, the line rows the pass wrote whose read_as is empty; fields_seen, the fields (agent, kind, path) its lines showed; fields_new, those it wrote a first line_field row for; and fields_not_stored, those of fields_seen that no column holds. status prints the lines source's line as `&lt;items&gt; read of &lt;lines_held&gt; held, &lt;lines_unused&gt; fed no table, &lt;unread&gt; in a shape it does not know · &lt;fields_seen&gt; fields, &lt;fields_new&gt; new, &lt;fields_not_stored&gt; not stored · &lt;rows&gt; rows`, its outcome after it as today; every other source's line stays as it is.</step>
    </module>
